Higher K K -theory of forms II. From exact categories to chain complexes
Marco Schlichting

Abstract
. We prove basic statements about the Hermitian K K -theory of exact form categories with weak equivalences providing the background for the comparison in [ MS ] of the classical 1 1 -categorical version of the Hermitian K K -theory of exact categories as defined in [ Sch21 ] and the ∞ \infty -categorical version of [ CDH+23 ] . Notably, in the current paper, we extend a quadratic functor with values in abelian groups from an exact category to its category of bounded chain complexes in a way that does not change Grothendieck-Witt spaces.



1.
Introduction

This is the second paper in the series
[
Sch21
]
,
[
Sch
]
,
[
MS
]
announced in
[
Sch21
]
which lead to
[
Sch19
]
,
[
Sch24
]
.
In
[
Sch21
]
we defined the Hermitian
K
-theory
GW(\mathscr{E},Q)
of an exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
and showed that if all admissible exact sequences in
\mathscr{E}
split, then that definition agrees with the group completion definition of quadratic spaces in
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)

[
Sch21
, Theorem 1.1]
.
This is the analog in Hermitian
K
-theory of Quillen’s
Q=+
Theorem
[
Gra76
, Theorem p.7]
.

In this paper, we will study the Hermitian
K
-theory of exact form categories with weak equivalences and prove basic results such as Additivity (Theorem
8.1
), a Fibration Theorem (Theorem
9.3
) and a Cofinality Theorem (Theorem
9.8
) the analogs of which are well-known in algebraic
K
-theory.
Our main result (Theorem
10.5
) associates to any exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
in the sense of
[
Sch21
]
, an exact form category with weak equivalences
(\operatorname{Ch}_{b}\mathscr{E},\operatorname{quis},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
of bounded chain complexes in
\mathscr{E}
and states that the inclusion
\mathscr{E}\subset\operatorname{Ch}_{b}\mathscr{E}
as complexes concentrated in degree
0
induces a homotopy equivalence of associated Grothendieck-Witt spaces

(1.1)

GW(\mathscr{E},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\operatorname{Ch}_{b}\mathscr{E},\operatorname{quis},Q).

This generalises
[
Sch10b
, Proposition 6]
from symmetric forms to arbitrary quadratic forms with values in abelian groups.
Our main contribution here is our definition of the extension of the quadratic functor
Q:\mathscr{E}^{op}\to\operatorname{Ab}
defined on an exact category
\mathscr{E}
to a quadratic functor
Q:(\operatorname{Ch}_{b}\mathscr{E})^{op}\to\operatorname{Ab}
defined on all bounded chain complexes in
\mathscr{E}
such that the homotopy equivalence (
1.1
) holds.

We note that results similar to our Additivity, Fibration and Cofinality Theorems have been obtained in
[
CDH+23
]
and its sequals in the setting of infinity categories.
To date, however, agreement of the approach of
[
CDH+23
]
with the classical
1
-categorical approach of
[
Sch21
]
has only been proved in special cases such as the split exact case
[
HS
]
,
[
Sch21
]
, the case when
2
is invertible
[
CDH+
, Corollary B.2.5]
, or the case when Zariski-descent holds
[
CHN
]
,
[
Sch10b
]
.
In
[
MS
]
,
we will associate to any complicial exact form category with weak equivalences a Poincare infinity category in the sense of
[
CDH+23
]
and show that the 1-categorical Hermitian
K
-theory of the former as defined in
[
Sch21
]
and this paper agrees with the infinity categorical Hermitian
K
-theory of the latter as defined in
[
CDH+23
]
.
Together with the homotopy equivalence (
1.1
) of Theorem
10.5
, this shows that the approach of
[
Sch21
]
agrees with that of
[
CDH+23
]
generalising all previous agreement statements cited above.

In addition to the results already mentioned, we will construct a sequence of (usually non-connective) spectra
GW^{[n]}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)
,
n\geq 0
, for any exact form category with weak equivalences and strong symmetric cone
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
whose negative homotopy groups
GW_{i}^{[n]}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)=\pi_{i}GW^{[n]}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)
,
i<0
, are the Witt groups
W(\mathscr{E}^{[n-i]},w,Q)
of certain exact form categories with weak equivalences
(\mathscr{E}^{[n]},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
, where
(\mathscr{E}^{[0]},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)=(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
.
The infinite loop space
\Omega^{\infty}GW^{[n]}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)
is the Grothendieck-Witt space
GW(\mathscr{E}^{[n]},w,Q)
, and the sequence of spectra fit into a homotopy fibration of spectra with connective algebraic
K
-theory for
n\geq 0

GW^{[n]}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\longrightarrow}}K(\mathscr{E},w)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW^{[n+1]}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)

generalising the Bott sequence of
[
Sch17
, Theorem 6.1]
.
This is proved in Theorem
11.9
.

Most of the results in this paper were obtained in conjuction with
[
Sch21
]
.
Often, the proofs are direct adaptations of the case of symmetric forms and are
mutatis mutandis
the same as in
[
Sch10a
]
,
[
Sch10b
]
and
[
Sch17
]
.
Our main contribution is to equip everything in sight with the correct quadratic functor.
At places, we have taken the opportunity to simplify proofs (
e.g.
, Theorem
3.1
), or to shorten proofs relying on the symmetric forms case (
e.g.
, Theorem
5.3
and Lemma
5.12
).

Part I
Exact form categories

2.
The form
Q
-construction

Recall
[
Sch10a
, Definition 2.1]
that
an
exact category with duality
is a tuple
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can})
where
\mathscr{E}
is an exact category
[
Qui73
]
,
\sharp:\mathscr{E}^{op}\to\mathscr{E}
is an exact functor,
\operatorname{can}:1\to\sharp\sharp
is a natural transformation such that
1_{X^{\sharp}}=\operatorname{can}_{X}^{\sharp}\operatorname{can}_{X^{\sharp}}
for all objects
X\in\mathscr{E}
.
We denote by
\sigma
the map

\sigma:\mathscr{E}(X,Y^{\sharp})\to\mathscr{E}(Y,X^{\sharp}):f\mapsto f^{\sharp}\operatorname{can}_{Y}

natural in
X
and
Y
.
Occasionally, we may write
\bar{f}
for
\sigma(f)
.
Note that
\sigma^{2}=1
.
We call the duality
strong
if
\operatorname{can}:1\to\sharp\sharp
is an isomorphism, and we call the duality
strict
if
X^{\sharp\sharp}=X
and
\operatorname{can}_{X}=1_{X}
for all objects
X
of
\mathscr{E}
.
Clearly, a strict duality is also strong.

Definition 2.1

(
[
Sch21
, Definitions 2.1 and 2.22]
1
1

1

In
[
Sch21
]
the functor
\sharp
was always (implicitly) assumed to be exact though this wasn’t stated explicitly in
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.22]
.
)
.

An
exact form category
is a tuple
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
where
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can})
is an exact category with duality and
Q:\mathscr{E}^{op}\to\operatorname{Ab}
is a functor with values in abelian groups together with a functorial
C_{2}
-equivariant diagram of abelian groups

(\mathscr{E}(X,X^{\sharp}),\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathscr{E}(X,X^{\sharp}),\sigma)

where
Q(X)
carries the trivial
C_{2}
-action.
We require the following.

(1)

\rho\tau=1+\sigma
.

(2)

For all
f,g\in\mathscr{E}(X,Y)
and
\xi\in Q(Y)
we have

(f+g)^{\bullet}(\xi)=f^{\bullet}(\xi)+g^{\bullet}(\xi)+\tau(g^{\sharp}\circ\rho(\xi)\circ f)

where
f^{\bullet}(\xi)
denotes
Q(f)(\xi)
.

(3)

For every admissible exact sequence
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}Z
in
\mathscr{E}
, the following sequence of abelian groups is exact

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 63.69507pt\raise 6.4221pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.061pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p^{\bullet}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.725pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 82.725pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 166.43613pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}(Y,X^{\sharp})\times Q(X)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The elements of
Q(X)
are called quadratic forms on
X
.

Definition 2.2
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can})
be an exact form category with strong duality.
A
quadratic space
2
2

2

The notion of quadratic space will be generalised in Definition
7.4
below.

in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
is a pair
(X,\xi)
where
X
is an object of
\mathscr{E}
and
\xi\in Q(X)
is a quadratic form on
X
such that
\rho(\xi)
is an isomorphism.
A map of quadratic spaces
f:(X,\xi)\to(Y,\zeta)
is a map
f:X\to Y
in
\mathscr{E}
such that
\xi=f^{\bullet}(\zeta)
.
Composition of maps of quadratic spaces is composition in
\mathscr{E}
.
An isomorphism of quadratic spaces is also called
isometry
.
We denote by

i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)

the category of quadratic spaces and isometries in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
.

Recall
[
Qui73
]
that Quillen associates to any exact category
\mathscr{E}
a new category
\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}
and defines the
K
-theory space of
\mathscr{E}
as
K(\mathscr{E})=\Omega|\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}|
, the loop space of the classifying space
|\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}|
of
\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}
.
The objects of
\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}
are the objects in
\mathscr{E}
and the arrows
X\to Y
are equivalences classes
[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y]
of data where
p
is an admissible epimorphism and
s
is an admissible monomorphism in
\mathscr{E}
.
We have equality of equivalence classes
[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y]=[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{\prime}}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y]
if and only if there is an isomorphism
u:U\cong U^{\prime}
such that
p^{\prime}=up
and
s^{\prime}=us
.
Composition is defined by

[Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Z]\circ[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y]=[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle pq^{\prime}}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\times_{Y}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle rs^{\prime}}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Z]

where
q^{\prime}:U\times_{Y}V\twoheadrightarrow U
and
s^{\prime}:U\times_{Y}V\rightarrowtail V
are the canonical projections.
Note that
[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y]=s_{!}\circ p^{!}
where
p^{!}=[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1}}{{\rightarrowtail}}U]
and
s_{!}=[U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y]
.
We have
s_{!}\circ p^{!}=q^{!}\circ t_{!}
where
V
is the pushout of
p
along
s
with
q:Y\twoheadrightarrow V
and
t:X\rightarrowtail V
the induced maps.

Definition 2.3

(The form
Q
-construction)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
We define a new category

\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)=\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q),

called
form
Q
-construction
, as follows.
Objects are the quadratic spaces in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
.
An arrow
(X,\xi)\to(Y,\zeta)
is an arrow
[X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadleftarrow}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y]
in Quillen’s
Q\mathscr{E}
such that

(1)

p^{\bullet}(\xi)=s^{\bullet}(\zeta)
and

(2)

\ker(p)=U^{\perp}
where
U^{\perp}=\ker(s^{\sharp}\rho(\zeta))
is the orthogonal of
U
in
Y
.

Composition is composition in Quillen’s
\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}
.
Note that (
2
) is equivalent to the square

\textstyle{U\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s^{\sharp}\rho(\zeta)}
\textstyle{X\ \ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\sharp}\rho(\xi)}
\textstyle{U^{\sharp}}

being bicartesian.

In
[
Sch21
, Definition 6.1]
, for every poset
\mathcal{P}
with strict duality
\mathcal{P}^{op}\to\mathcal{P}:i\mapsto i^{\prime}
and every exact form category
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
, we have equipped the category of functors
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{P},\mathscr{E})
with the structure
(\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
of an exact form category.
The dual of a functor
A:\mathcal{P}\to\mathscr{E}
is
(A^{\sharp})_{i}=(A_{i^{\prime}})^{\sharp}
.
A
quadratic form
on a functor
A:\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{A}:i\mapsto A_{i}
is a pair
(\xi,\varphi)
where
\xi=(\xi_{i})_{i\leq i^{\prime}}
is a family of quadratic forms
\xi_{i}\in Q(A_{i})
indexed over those
i\in\mathcal{P}
satisfying
i\leq i^{\prime}
, and
\varphi:A\to A^{\sharp}
is a symmetric form in
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{A})
such that
A_{i\leq j}^{\bullet}(\xi_{j})=\xi_{i}
whenever
i\leq j\leq j^{\prime}\leq i^{\prime}
, and
\rho(\xi_{i})=\varphi_{i^{\prime}}\circ A_{i\leq i^{\prime}}
.
In particular, the categories
S_{n}\mathscr{E}
of Waldhausen
[
Wal85
]
are equipped with the structure
(\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
of an exact form category as a fully exact form subcategory of

\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Ar}([n]),\mathscr{E})
where
[n]=\{0<1<\dots n\}
has unique strict duality
i\mapsto n-i
; see
[
Sch21
, §6]
.

Example 2.4
.

Let us make the structure of the exact form category on
S_{2}\mathscr{E}
more explicit as this will be needed in the Additivity Theorem
3.1
below.
Similar observations can be made for
S_{n}\mathscr{E}
.
Let
(\mathscr{E},\ast,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category.
Objects of
S_{n}\mathscr{E}
are functors
A:\operatorname{Ar}[n]\to\mathscr{E}:(i\leq j)\mapsto A_{ij}
such that
A_{ii}=0
and
A_{ij}\rightarrowtail A_{ik}\twoheadrightarrow A_{jk}
is admissible exact whenever
i\leq j\leq k
.

The exact category
S_{2}\mathscr{E}
is therefore the category of admissible exact sequences
A_{01}\rightarrowtail A_{02}\twoheadrightarrow A_{12}
in
\mathscr{E}
with point-wise exact structure.
In
\operatorname{Ar}[2]
, only the pairs
(i,j)
of the form
(0,0)
,
(0,1)
,
(0,2)
,
(1,1)
satisfy
(i,j)\leq(i,j)^{\prime}=(2-j,2-i)
.
Thus, to give a quadratic form on
A\in S_{2}\mathscr{E}
is the same as to give a symmetric bilinear form
\varphi:A\to A^{*}
and quadratic forms
\xi_{0,0}\in Q(A_{0,0})
,
\xi_{0,1}\in Q(A_{0,1})
,
\xi_{1,1}\in Q(A_{1,1})
,
\xi_{1,2}\in Q(A_{1,2})
satisfying the compatibilities of
[
Sch21
, Definition 6.1]
recalled above.
Since
A_{0,0}=A_{1,1}=0
, we have
\xi_{0,0}=\xi_{1,1}=0
.
Since
(0,1)\leq(1,1),(0,2)
, the form
\xi_{0,1}
is the restriction of
\xi_{0,2}
and of
\xi_{1,1}=0
.
In particular,
0=\xi_{0,1}=(\xi_{0,2})_{|A_{0,1}}
.
Moreover,
\rho(\xi_{0,2})=\varphi_{0,2}
.
Note that
\varphi_{1,2}=\varphi_{0,1}\circ\operatorname{can}_{1,2}
, that
\varphi_{0,1}
is determined by
\varphi_{0,2}
and that
\varphi_{0,1}
exists if and only if

A_{(0,1)\to(0,2)}^{*}\circ\varphi_{0,2}\circ A_{(0,1)\to(0,2)}=0
.

Thus,
S_{2}\mathscr{E}
is the category of admissible exact sequences
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y\twoheadrightarrow Z
in
\mathscr{E}
with point-wise exact structure.
A quadratic form on such an object is a quadratic form
\xi\in Q(Y)
such that
a^{\bullet}\xi=0
.
Its associated symmetric form is determined by
\rho(\xi):Y\to Y^{*}
.
Indeed, it satisfies
a^{*}\circ\rho(\xi)\circ a=\rho(a^{\bullet}\xi)=0
and thus defines a symmetric form on the exact sequence.
If
\mathscr{E}
has a strong duality, then the form
\xi
on
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y\twoheadrightarrow Z
is non-degenerate if and only if the map of exact sequences induced by
\rho(\xi):Y\to Y^{*}
is an isomorphism.
The latter is equivalent to
\rho(\xi):Y\to Y^{*}
being an isomorphism such that

(a,a^{*}\rho(\xi))
are the maps in an admissible exact sequence
X\rightarrowtail Y\twoheadrightarrow X^{*}
.
Note that, up to equivalence of form categories, we do not need to specify the quotient
Z
is it is determined up to isomorphism by the admissible monomorphism
a:X\rightarrowtail Y
.

For a simplicial object
X
, denote by
X^{e}
the edge-wise subdivision
[
Wal85
, §1.9]
,
[
Sch21
, §6]
.
Its object of
n
-simplicies is
X^{e}[n]=X([n]^{op}[n])
where
[n]^{op}[n]
is the totally ordered set
\{n^{op}<\dots<1^{op}<0^{op}<0<\dots n\}
.
The natural inclusion
[n]\to[n]^{op}[n]:i\mapsto i
induces the natural transformation of simplicial objects
X^{e}\to X
which induces a weak equivalence
|X^{e}|\to|X|
of topological realisations for all simplicial sets
X

[
Sch10b
, Lemma 1]
.

Recall
[
Sch21
, Definition 6.3]
that the Grothendieck-Witt space of an exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
is defined as the space
GW(\mathscr{E},Q)
which sits in a homotopy fibration

(2.1)

GW(\mathscr{E},Q)\longrightarrow|i\operatorname{Quad}(S_{\bullet}^{e}\mathscr{E},Q)|\longrightarrow|iS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}|

where the second map is the composition of the map
i\operatorname{Quad}(S_{\bullet}^{e}\mathscr{E},Q)\to iS^{e}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}:(A,\xi)\mapsto A
forgetting quadratic forms followed by the natural transformation
iS^{e}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}\to iS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}
.
Since
S_{0}\mathscr{E}=0
and
S_{1}\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{E}
, the natural zero simplex inclusion
X^{e}[0]=X_{1}\to X^{e}

induces, for
X=i\operatorname{Quad}(S_{\bullet}^{e}\mathscr{E},Q)
, a map

|i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)|\to GW(\mathscr{E},Q).

If all admissible exact sequences in
\mathscr{E}
split then this map is a group completion of the homotopy commutative
H
-space
(|i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)|,\perp)
; see
[
Sch21
, Theorem 6.6]
.

Below, we will need to iterate the
S^{e}_{\bullet}
-construction.
To avoid awkward notation, we may write
\mathcal{R}_{n}
for
S_{n}^{e}
.
So

\mathcal{R}_{n}\mathscr{E}=S^{e}_{n}\mathscr{E}.

In this notation, the homotopy fibration (
2.1
)
becomes the homotopy fibration

(2.2)

GW(\mathscr{E},Q)\longrightarrow|i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)|\longrightarrow|iS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}|.

Proposition 2.5
.

For any exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
, there is natural zigzag of
homotopy
equivalences between the following two topological spaces

|\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)|\sim|i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)|.

Proof.

The proof is
mutatis mutandis
the same as in
[
Sch10b
, Proposition 2]
, and we omit the details.
∎

Remark 2.6
.

As in
[
Sch10b
, Proposition 2]
, Proposition
2.5
implies that there is a homotopy fibration

GW(\mathscr{E},Q)\to\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)\to\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E},

but we won’t need this.

Definition 2.7

(The form category of exact functors)
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},*,\operatorname{can}_{\mathcal{A}},Q_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},*,\operatorname{can}_{\mathcal{B}},Q_{\mathcal{B}})
be two small exact form categories.
We make the category of exact functors
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})

into an exact form category

(2.3)

(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}),\sharp,\eta,Q)

as follows.
For exact functors
F,G:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
, denote by
[F,G]
the set of maps from
F
to
G
in the category
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
, that is, the set of natural transformations
F\to G
.

(1)

A sequence
F_{0}\to F_{1}\to F_{2}
of functors
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is called admissible exact if for all objects
A\in\mathcal{A}
the sequence
F_{0}A\rightarrowtail F_{1}A\twoheadrightarrow F_{2}A
is admissible exact in
\mathcal{B}
.

(2)

The dual of a functor
F:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is
F^{\sharp}=\ast F\ast
.
The dual of a natural transformation
f:F\to G
is the natural transformation
f^{\sharp}:G^{\sharp}\to F^{\sharp}
which at
A\in\mathcal{A}
is

(f^{\sharp})_{A}=(f_{A^{*}})^{*}
.
The double dual identification
\eta_{F}:F\to F^{\sharp\sharp}
at
A\in\mathcal{A}
is

(\eta_{F})_{A}=\operatorname{can}_{F(A^{**})}\circ F(\operatorname{can}_{A}):FA\to F^{\sharp\sharp}A=F(A^{**})^{**}
.

(3)

There is an isomorphism of symmetric bilinear functors

\hat{\phantom{f}}:[F,G^{\sharp}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\longrightarrow}}[F*,*G]:g\mapsto\hat{g}=(G\operatorname{can})^{*}\circ g_{*}

with inverse
the map
f\mapsto f_{*}\circ F(\operatorname{can})
,
where the symmetry on the right is

[F*,*G]\to[G*,*F]:\varphi\mapsto\tilde{\varphi}=(F\operatorname{can})^{*}\circ\varphi_{*}^{*}\circ\operatorname{can}_{G*}.

We have
\tilde{\tilde{\varphi}}=\varphi
and
\varphi^{*}_{A}\circ\operatorname{can}_{GA}=\tilde{\varphi}_{A^{*}}\circ G(\operatorname{can}_{A})
for all
A\in\mathcal{A}
.

(4)

For any exact functor
F:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
, any natural transformation
\varphi:F*\to*F
and any arrow
a:A\to A^{*}
in
\mathcal{A}
we have

\overline{\varphi_{A}\circ F(a)}=\tilde{\varphi}_{A}\circ F(\bar{a}).

(5)

On objects
G\in\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
, the set
Q(G)
of quadratic forms on the exact functor
G:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
is the set

Q(G)=\{(q,\varphi)|\ (G,q,\varphi):(\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\to(\mathcal{B},Q_{\mathcal{B}})\ \text{exact form functor}\}

of pairs
(q,\varphi)

of natural transformations
q:Q_{\mathcal{A}}\to Q_{\mathcal{B}}G
and
\varphi:G\to G^{\sharp}

such that
(G,q,\varphi)
is an exact form functor
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.6]
from
(\mathcal{A},*,\operatorname{can}_{\mathcal{A}},Q_{\mathcal{A}})
to
(\mathcal{B},*,\operatorname{can}_{\mathcal{B}},Q_{\mathcal{B}})
.
Recall that this means that
\varphi^{\sharp}\eta_{G}=\varphi
, and the following diagram commutes for all
A\in\mathcal{A}

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}(A,A^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\varphi}_{A}\circ G(\phantom{a})}
\textstyle{Q(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\rho}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}(A,A^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hat{\varphi}_{A}\circ G(\phantom{a})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}(GA,\ast GA)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 6.02777pt\tau}
\textstyle{Q(GA)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-9.04166pt\rho}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}(GA,\ast GA).}

On morphisms
f:F\to G
in
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
, the map
Q(f):Q(G)\to Q(F)
sends
(q,\varphi)\in Q(G)
to
(Q_{\mathcal{B}}(f)\circ q,f^{\sharp}\varphi f)\in Q(F)
.

(6)

For
G\in\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
, the functorial Mackey-functor of quadratic forms

[G,G^{\sharp}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}[G,G^{\sharp}]

has restriction
\rho(q,\varphi)=\varphi
and transfer
\tau(\varphi)=(q,\varphi+\varphi^{\sharp}\eta_{G})
, where the natural transformation
q:Q_{\mathcal{A}}\to Q_{\mathcal{B}}G
is defined by

q_{A}(\xi)=\tau(\hat{\varphi}_{A}\circ G\rho(\xi))\ \in Q_{\mathcal{B}}(GA),

for
A\in\mathcal{A}
and
\xi\in Q_{\mathcal{A}}(A)
.

Lemma 2.8
.

The tuple (
2.3
) is an exact form category.

Proof.

It is clear that the triple
(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}),\sharp,\operatorname{can})
is an exact category with duality and that Definition
2.1
(
1
) holds.
In order to verify Definition
2.1
(
2
), we let
f,g:F\to G
be two natural transformations of exact functors
F,G:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
.
Then, for
(q,\varphi)\in Q(G)
, we have to check the equality

(2.4)

[Q(f+g)-Q(f)-Q(g)](q,\varphi)=\tau(g^{\sharp}\circ\rho(q,\varphi)\circ f)\ \ \in Q(F).

The left hand side is

\begin{array}[]{rl}&(Q_{\mathcal{B}}(f+g)q-Q_{\mathcal{B}}(f)q-Q_{\mathcal{B}}(g)q,\ (f+g)^{\sharp}\ \varphi\ (f+g)-f^{\sharp}\varphi f-g^{\sharp}\varphi g)\\
=&(\tau(g^{*}\ \rho(q)\ f),\ f^{\sharp}\varphi g+g^{\sharp}\varphi f),\end{array}

whereas the right hand side of (
2.4
) is

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\tau(g^{\sharp}\varphi f)&=&(\tau(\widehat{g^{\sharp}\varphi f}\circ F(\rho)),\ g^{\sharp}\varphi f+(g^{\sharp}\varphi f)^{\sharp}\eta_{F})\\
&=&(\tau[(F\operatorname{can})^{*}\circ g^{\sharp}_{*}\varphi_{*}f_{*}\circ F(\rho)],g^{\sharp}\varphi f+f^{\sharp}\varphi g)\end{array}

in view of
(g^{\sharp}\varphi f)^{\sharp}\eta_{F}=f^{\sharp}\varphi^{\sharp}g^{\sharp\sharp}\eta_{F}=f^{\sharp}(\varphi^{\sharp}\eta_{G})g
and
\varphi^{\sharp}\eta_{G}=\varphi
.
In particular, the two expressions in (
2.4
) agree on the second component.
At
A\in\mathcal{A}
and
\xi\in Q_{\mathcal{A}}(\xi)
, the first component of the left hand side of (
2.4
) is
\tau_{FA}
applied to

g^{*}_{A}\circ\rho(q_{A}(\xi))\circ f_{A}=g^{*}_{A}\circ\hat{\varphi}_{A}\circ G(\rho(\xi))\circ f_{A}=g^{*}_{A}\circ G(\operatorname{can}_{A})^{*}\varphi_{A^{*}}\circ G(\rho(\xi))\circ f_{A},

whereas the first component of the right hand side of (
2.4
) is
\tau_{FA}
applied to

(F\operatorname{can}_{A})^{*}\circ g^{\sharp}_{A^{*}}\varphi_{A^{*}}f_{A^{*}}\circ F(\rho(\xi)).

The last two expressions agree since we have

G(\rho(\xi))\circ f_{A}=f_{A^{*}}\circ F(\rho(\xi))
and
g^{*}_{A}\circ G(\operatorname{can}_{A})^{*}=(F\operatorname{can}_{A})^{*}\circ g^{\sharp}_{A^{*}}
in view of the naturality of
f
and
g
.

Finally, we need to check condition (
3
) of Definition
2.1
, that is, for an exact sequence of functors
0\to F_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\to}}F_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\to}}F_{3}\to 0
from
A
to
\mathcal{B}
, we have to verify the exactness of the sequence

(2.5)

0\to Q(F_{3})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Q(g)}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q(F_{2})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(Q(f),\rho\circ f)}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q(F_{1})\times[F_{1},F_{2}^{\sharp}].

The map
Q(g)
is injective because its component functions
Q_{\mathcal{B}}(g)
and
\varphi\mapsto g^{\sharp}\varphi g
are injective as
g
is an admissible epimorphism.
For exactness at
Q(F_{2})
, it is clear that the composition of the two maps in (
2.5
) is zero.
Now consider
(q,\varphi)\in Q(F_{2})
such that
0=(Q_{\mathcal{B}}(f)\circ q,f^{\sharp}\varphi f)\in Q(F_{1})
and
0=\varphi f\in[F_{1},F_{2}^{\sharp}]
.
Then there is a unique natural map
\psi:F_{3}\to F_{3}^{\sharp}
such that
g^{\sharp}\psi g=\varphi
since
F_{3}
is a cokernel of
f
and
F_{3}^{\sharp}
is a kernel of
f^{\sharp}
.
By uniqueness and the equation
\varphi^{\sharp}\eta_{F_{2}}=\varphi
, we have
\psi^{\sharp}\eta_{F_{3}}=\psi
.
In view of the exact sequence

0\to Q_{\mathcal{B}}(F_{3}A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Q_{\mathcal{B}}(g)}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{B}}(F_{2}A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(Q_{\mathcal{B}}(f),\rho\circ f)}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{B}}(F_{1}A)\times\mathcal{B}(F_{1}A,(F_{2}A)^{*})

for
A\in\mathcal{A}
,
the natural transformation
q:Q_{\mathcal{A}}\to Q_{\mathcal{B}}\circ F_{2}
uniquely factors through
Q_{\mathcal{B}}(g):Q_{\mathcal{B}}\circ F_{3}\hookrightarrow Q_{\mathcal{B}}\circ F_{2}
since
0=Q_{\mathcal{B}}(f)\circ q
, by assumption, and for
\xi\in Q_{\mathcal{A}}(A)
we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\rho_{F_{2}A}(q_{A}(\xi))\circ f_{A}&=&\hat{\varphi}_{A}\circ F_{2}(\rho_{A}\xi)\circ f_{A}\\
&=&F_{2}(\operatorname{can}_{A})^{*}\circ\varphi_{A^{*}}\circ F_{2}(\rho_{A}\xi)\circ f_{A}\\
&=&F_{2}(\operatorname{can}_{A})^{*}\circ F_{2}^{\sharp}(\rho_{A}\xi)\circ\varphi_{A}\circ f_{A}\\
&=&0\end{array}

as
\varphi_{A}f_{A}=0
.
Hence, there is a unique natural transformation
r:Q_{\mathcal{A}}\to Q_{\mathcal{B}}\circ F_{3}
such that
Q_{\mathcal{B}}(g)\circ r=q
.
Then
(r,\psi)\in Q(F_{3})
with
Q(g)(r,\psi)=(q,\varphi)
.
Indeed, the diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}(A,A^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\psi}_{A}\circ F_{3}(\phantom{a})}
\textstyle{Q_{\mathcal{A}}(A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{A}}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{A}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}(A,A^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widehat{\psi}_{A}\circ F_{3}(\phantom{a})}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}(F_{3}A,\ast F_{3}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 6.02777pt\tau_{F_{3}A}}
\textstyle{Q_{\mathcal{B}}(F_{3}A)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-9.04166pt\rho_{F_{3}A}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}(F_{3}A,\ast F_{3}A),}

when composed with the injective map to
(\tau_{F_{2}A},\rho_{F_{2}A})
with components
\mathcal{B}(g,g^{*})
,
Q_{\mathcal{B}}(g)
and
\mathcal{B}(g,g^{*})
, yields the map from
(\tau_{A},\rho_{A})
to
(\tau_{F_{2}A},\rho_{F_{2}A})
with components
\hat{\varphi}_{A}\circ F_{2}
,
q_{A}
and
\hat{\varphi}_{A}\circ F_{2}
.
∎

Remark 2.9

(Terminology)
.

According to Lemma
2.8
, if
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are exact form categories then so is
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
.
Note that if the dualities on
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are strong then so is the duality on
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
.
In this case, a non-singular exact form functor from
\mathcal{A}
to
\mathcal{B}
, that is, a non-singular form functor
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.5]
which is exact, is the same is quadratic space in
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
, see Definition
2.7
.
Therefore, any standard fact and terminology that apply to any exact form category with strong duality also apply to
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
.
For instance, we have the notion of a
hyperbolic exact form functor
, that is, a hyperbolic object in
(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}),\sharp,\eta,Q)
.
We have the notion of a
metabolic exact form functor
, that is, a metabolic object in
(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}),\sharp,\eta,Q)
.
Similarly, we have the notions of a
Lagrangian and a sublagrangian functor of an exact form functor

[
Sch21
, Definition 2.24]
,
othogonal decompositions
of non-singular exact form functors
[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.28]
,
sublagrangian construction

[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.29]
…

Lemma 2.10
.

Let

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{D}

be a bicartesian square in an exact form category
(\mathscr{E},\ast,\operatorname{can},Q)
,
that is,
the following sequence is admissible exact

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-9.04166pt\left(\begin{smallmatrix}s\\
-f\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{B\oplus C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 6.02777pt(g\ t)}
\textstyle{D\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Then the following square of abelian groups is cartesian (that is, a pull-back)

\textstyle{Q(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}g^{\bullet}\\
t^{\bullet}\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\scriptstyle{t^{*}\circ\rho(\phantom{x})\circ g}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}(B,C^{*})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q(B)\oplus Q(C)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q(A)\oplus\mathscr{E}(B,A^{*})\oplus\mathscr{E}(A,C^{*})}

where the right vertical and lower horizontal maps are the obvious one’s (no sign):

\left(\begin{matrix}\tau(f^{*}\circ(\phantom{x})\circ s)\\
f^{*}(\phantom{x})\\
(\phantom{x})\circ s\end{matrix}\right)\hskip 12.91663pt\text{and}\hskip 12.91663pt\left(\begin{matrix}s^{\bullet}&f^{\bullet}\\
s^{*}\circ\rho(\phantom{x})&0\\
0&\rho(\phantom{x})\circ f\end{matrix}\right)

Proof.

This is a consequence of Definition
2.1
(
3
).
∎

3.
Additivity for exact form categories

Hyperbolic and forgetful form functors
.
For any exact category
\mathscr{E}
we have defined in
[
Sch10a
, §3.5]
an exact category with strict duality
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
, called the
hyperbolic category
of
\mathscr{E}
, whose
GW
-space is equivalent to the
K
-theory space of
\mathscr{E}
.
As exact category,
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is
\mathscr{E}\times\mathscr{E}^{op}
, and the duality functor is
(X,Y)\mapsto(Y,X)
.
Any exact category with duality
(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can})
can be considered as an exact form category
(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
where
Q(X)=\mathcal{A}(X,X^{\sharp})^{C_{2}}
is the abelian group of symmetric forms on
X
; see
[
Sch21
, Example 2.24]
.
In this sense,
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
is considered an exact form category.
Recall from
[
Sch10a
, Remark 4.3 and Proposition 4.7]
the homotopy equivalences

\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E})\simeq\mathcal{Q}(\mathscr{E})\hskip 12.91663pt\text{and}\hskip 12.91663ptGW(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E})\simeq K(\mathscr{E})

where the first is the equivalence of categories
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E})\simeq\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}:(X,Y,\varphi)\mapsto X
.

For any exact form category
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
we have exact form functors

\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},

called
hyperbolic form functor
and
forgetful form functor
, respectively.
The hyperbolic form functor
H
sends the object
(X,Y)
to
X\oplus Y^{\sharp}
, an arrow
(f,g)
to
f\oplus g^{\sharp}
, has duality compatibility
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
\operatorname{can}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right):Y\oplus X^{\sharp}\to X^{\sharp}\oplus Y^{\sharp\sharp}
, and on quadratic forms it is
the composition

\textstyle{Q_{\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}}(X,Y)=\mathscr{E}(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f\mapsto\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0\\
\operatorname{can}_{Y}f&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)}
\textstyle{\mathscr{E}(X\oplus Y^{\sharp},X^{\sharp}\oplus Y^{\sharp\sharp})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.06943pt\tau}
\textstyle{Q_{\mathscr{E}}(X\oplus Y^{\sharp}).}

The forgetful form functor
F
sends the object
X
of
\mathscr{E}
to
(X,X^{\sharp})
, the arrow
f
of
\mathscr{E}
to
(f,f^{\sharp})
, has duality compatibility
(1,\operatorname{can}):(X^{\sharp},X^{\sharp\sharp})\to(X^{\sharp},X)
, and on quadratic forms it is the map

Q_{\mathscr{E}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}}(X,X^{\sharp})=\mathscr{E}(X,X^{\sharp}).

For any exact form category
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
we define the exact form functors

(3.1)

(S_{2}\mathscr{E},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}(S_{2}\mathscr{E})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{ev}_{01}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}

where the second functor is obtained from the evaluation
S_{2}\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{E}:X\mapsto X_{01}
at the object
(0,1)\in\operatorname{Ar}[2]
by functoriality of the hyperbolic category construction.

Theorem 3.1

(Additivity for exact categories)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},*,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
Then the following functor is a weak equivalence

\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(S_{2}\mathscr{E},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}:(X,\xi)\mapsto X_{01}.

In particular, the form functor (
3.1
)
induces a weak equivalence of spaces

GW(S_{2}\mathscr{E},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E})\simeq K(\mathscr{E}).

Proof.

We will write
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(S_{2}\mathscr{E})
for
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(S_{2}\mathscr{E},Q)
omitting in the notation the quadratic functor
Q
.
In view of the description of the exact form category
(S_{2}\mathscr{E},Q)
given in Example
2.4
,
the category
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}S_{2}\mathscr{E}
is equivalent to the category of tuples
(a:A_{0}\rightarrowtail A,\alpha)
where
(A,\alpha)
is a quadratic space in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
with Lagriangian
a:A_{0}\rightarrowtail A
.
Recall
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.5]
that the latter means that
a^{\bullet}(\alpha)=0
, and the admissible monomorphism
a:A_{0}\rightarrowtail A
induces an isomorphism
A_{0}\cong\ker(a^{*}\circ\varphi)
where
\varphi=\rho(\alpha)
.
An arrow
(a:A_{0}\rightarrowtail A,\alpha)\to(b:B_{0}\rightarrowtail B,\beta)
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}S_{2}\mathscr{E}
is an arrow in Quillen’s
\mathcal{Q}S_{2}\mathscr{E}
from the exact sequence
(a,a^{*}\varphi)
to
(b,b^{*}\psi)
represented by a diagram

(3.2)

\textstyle{A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a}
\textstyle{C_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hskip 4.30554pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{c}
\scriptstyle{j_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a^{*}\varphi}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hskip 4.30554pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{b^{*}\psi}
\textstyle{A_{0}^{*}}
\textstyle{C_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hskip 4.30554pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{j_{1}}
\textstyle{B_{0}^{*}}

where the columns are admissible exact sequences and
\psi=\rho(\beta)
.
The diagram defines a map in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}S_{2}\mathscr{E}
if and only if

j^{\bullet}(\beta)=p^{\bullet}(\alpha)
, and the following two squares are bicartesian

\textstyle{(x)}
\textstyle{C\hskip 4.30554pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{*}\psi}
\textstyle{(y)}
\textstyle{C_{0}\hskip 4.30554pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{0}}
\scriptstyle{p_{0}}
\textstyle{B_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{1}^{*}\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{A\hskip 4.30554pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{C^{*},}
\textstyle{A_{0}\hskip 4.30554pt\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{*}_{1}\operatorname{can}}
\scriptstyle{\Box}
\textstyle{C_{1}^{*}.}

Composition is composition in
{\mathcal{Q}}S_{2}\mathscr{E}
.

We want to show that the functor

G:{\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}}S_{2}\mathscr{E}\to\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}:(a:A_{0}\rightarrowtail A,\alpha)\mapsto A_{0}

is a weak equivalence.
We will use Quillen’s Theorem A and show that for every object
X\in\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}
, the comma category

\mathcal{C}=(G\downarrow X)
is non-empty and contractible.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}
be the full subcategory of objects
\mathcal{M}=(M,f:GM\to X)
such that
f=p^{!}
for some admissible epimorphism
p:X\twoheadrightarrow GM
in
\mathscr{E}
.
The category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
has an initial object, namely
(0,0^{!}:0\to X)
.
Hence
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is contractible (and
\mathcal{C}
is non-empty).
We will show that the inclusion
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}
has a left adjoint and hence is a weak equivalence finishing the proof.
To show the existence of a left adjoint, we have to construct for every object
\mathcal{M}\in\mathcal{C}
a universal arrow
u:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
in
\mathcal{C}
with
\mathcal{N}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, that is, an arrow
u:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
with target in
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
such that for every
\mathcal{M}_{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
every arrow
g:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{M}_{0}
in
\mathcal{C}
factors uniquely through
u
.

Given an object
\mathcal{M}=(M,f:GM\to X)
in
\mathcal{C}
where
M=(k:K\rightarrowtail M,\alpha)
is an object of
{\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}}S_{2}\mathscr{E}
, and
f:GM=K\to X
is a map in
\mathcal{Q}\mathscr{E}
.
The map
f
has a representation as
f=e^{!}\circ t_{!}
where
t:K\rightarrowtail L
is an admissible monomorphism and
e:X\twoheadrightarrow L
is an admissible epimorphism in
\mathscr{E}
.
We construct the object

\mathcal{N}=(N,e^{!}:GN=L\to X)

of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
as follows.
It consists of the object
N=(l:L\rightarrowtail N,\beta)
of
{\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}}S_{2}\mathscr{E}
with quadratic form
\beta\in Q(N)
, associated symmetric form
\rho(\beta)=\psi
and Lagrangian
l:L\subset N
.
Their definitions can be read off the diagrams below where a
\Box
denotes a bicartesian square:

In detail, let
P
be the pull-back of
k^{*}\varphi
along the admissible epimorphism
t^{*}
, and denote by
r:P\to L^{*}
and
m:P\to M
the induced maps; see the upper right part of the left diagram.
The maps
r
and
m
are admissible epimorphisms, since
k^{*}\varphi
and
t^{*}
are.
Since
(k,k^{*}\varphi)
is an admissible exact sequence, there is a unique admissible monomorphism
s:K\to P
such that
k=ms
and
(s,r)
is an admissible exact sequence.
Since
\varphi^{*}\operatorname{can}=\varphi
, the left diagram commutes.
Let
N
be the push-out of
s
along
t
with induced maps
n:P\to N
and
l:L\to N
being admissible monomorphisms since
s
and
t
are.
The dual of the push-out square is a pull-back square, and we obtain a unique map
\psi:N\to N^{*}
such that
n^{*}\psi\ n=m^{*}\varphi\ m
and
l^{*}\psi\ l=0
; making the right diagram commute.
The map
\psi
is an isomorphism, by the Five Lemma, since it is part of the map of admissible exact sequences
(1,\psi,1)
from
(l,l^{*}\psi)
to
(\psi l,l^{*})
.
It satisfies
\psi^{*}\operatorname{can}=\psi
, by uniqueness of
\psi
and the symmetry of the right diagram, comparing that diagram with its dual.
Lemma
2.10
applied to the cocartesian square defining
N
(upper left square in the right diagram) implies that there is a unique form
\beta\in Q(N)
such that

l^{*}\circ\rho(\beta)\circ n=r,\hskip 8.61108ptn^{\bullet}(\beta)=m^{\bullet}(\alpha),\hskip 8.61108ptl^{\bullet}(\beta)=0.

By the uniqueness of
\psi
satisfying the same equations (Lemma
2.10
appied to symmetric forms), it follows that
\rho(\beta)=\psi
, and
\beta
is non-degenerate.
Hence,
\mathcal{N}
is an object of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.

Thus, we have to show

\begin{array}[]{cccc}(1)&j_{M}^{\bullet}\ p_{N}^{\bullet}(\beta)&=&j_{M}^{\bullet}\ j_{N}^{\bullet}(\alpha_{0})\\

=&k_{0}^{*}\ \varphi_{0}\ j=k_{2}^{*}\ j_{N}^{*}\ \varphi_{0}\ j_{N}\ j_{M}=k_{2}^{*}\circ\rho(j_{N}^{\bullet}(\alpha_{0}))\circ j_{M}.\end{array}

since

,

,

, and

.

∎

For any
i\leq j
, the map
[0]\to\operatorname{Ar}[n]:0\mapsto(i\leq j)
induces by restriction
the exact evaluation functor
S_{n}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}:A\mapsto A_{ij}
evaluating at
i\leq j
.
The map
[0]\to\operatorname{Ar}[n]:0\mapsto(i\leq j)
preserves dualities if
j=n-i
in which case restriction along
[0]\to\operatorname{Ar}[n]
defines an exact form functor
(S_{n}\mathcal{A},Q)\to(\mathcal{A},Q):A\mapsto A_{ij}
.
For a form functor
G:(\mathcal{A},Q)\to S_{n}(\mathcal{B},Q)
, we will denote by
G_{ij}:(\mathcal{A},Q)\to(\mathcal{B},Q)
the composition of
G
and evaluation at
i\leq j
if
j=n-i
.
For an exact functor
G:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
we denote by
H(G):(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)\to(\mathcal{B},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
the hyperbolic form functor, that is, the form functor corresponding to the hyperbolic form on
G
in the exact form category
\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
of exact functors
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
of Lemma
2.8
.
This is also the composition

(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathscr{H}(G)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}\mathcal{B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathcal{B},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q).

A direct definition can be found in
[
Sch21
, Example 2.13]
.

Theorem 3.2

(Additivity for functors)
.

Let
(A,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
and
(\mathcal{B},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be exact form categories with strong duality.

(1)

Let
G=(G,\varphi_{q},\varphi):(\mathcal{A},Q)\to(S_{2}\mathcal{B},Q)
be a non-singular exact form functor.
Then the two non-singular exact form functors

G_{02}
and
H(G_{01})

induce homotopic maps of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

G_{02}\sim H(G_{01}):GW(\mathcal{A},Q)\to GW(\mathcal{B},Q).

(2)

Let
G=(G,\varphi_{q},\varphi):(\mathcal{A},Q)\to(S_{3}\mathcal{B},Q)
be a non-singular exact form functor.
Then the two non-singular exact form functors

G_{03}
and
G_{12}\perp H(G_{01})

induce homotopic maps of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

G_{03}\sim(G_{12}\perp H(G_{01})):GW(\mathcal{A},Q)\to GW(\mathcal{B},Q).

Proof.

This is
mutatis mutandis
the same as
[
Sch21
, Proposition 6.7]
replacing
[
Sch21
, Theorem 5.1]
with Theorem
3.1
. Note that the Grothendieck-Witt spaces of exact form categories with strong duality are group complete commutative
H
-spaces, by
[
Sch21
, Theorem 6.5]
.
∎

Corollary 3.3
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
Then the form functors

\begin{array}[]{ll}(S_{2n}\mathcal{A},Q)\to(\mathscr{H}\mathcal{A})^{n}:&(A,\xi)\mapsto H(A_{01},A_{12},...,A_{n-1,n})\\
(S_{2n+1}\mathcal{A},Q)\to(\mathscr{H}\mathcal{A})^{n}\times(\mathcal{A},Q):&(A,\xi)\mapsto H(A_{01},A_{12},...,A_{n-1,n}),(A_{n,n+1},\xi_{n,n+1})\end{array}

induce homotopy equivalences of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

GW(S_{2n}\mathcal{A},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\mathscr{H}\mathcal{A})^{n}\simeq K(\mathcal{A})^{n}

GW(S_{2n+1}\mathcal{A},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\mathscr{H}\mathcal{A})^{n}\times GW(\mathcal{A},Q)\simeq K(\mathcal{A})^{n}\times GW(\mathcal{A},Q).

Proof.

More precisely, the first functor is the forgetful functor
F:(S_{2n}\mathcal{A},Q)\to\mathscr{H}(S_{2n}\mathcal{A})
followed by
\mathscr{H}
applied to the exact functor
A\mapsto(A_{01},A_{12},...,A_{n-1,n})
.
The second functor on the first
n
factors is the forgetful functor
F:(S_{2n+1}\mathcal{A},Q)\to\mathscr{H}(S_{2n+1}\mathcal{A})
followed by
\mathscr{H}
applied to the exact functor
A\mapsto(A_{01},A_{12},...,A_{n-1,n})
, and on the last factor it is the evaluation
(A,\xi)\mapsto(A_{n,n+1},\xi_{n,n+1})
.
The corollary immediately follows from Theorem
3.2
and is
mutatis mutandis

the same as
[
Sch17
, Proposition A.8]
.
∎

4.
Cofinality for exact form categories

A full inclusion
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
of exact categories is called
fully exact
if it is extension closed, preserves and detects admissible exact sequences.
It is called
cofinal
if it is fully exact and for every object
X
of
\mathcal{U}
there is an object
Y
of
\mathcal{U}
such that
X\oplus Y
is isomorphic to an object of
\mathcal{A}
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
Let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be a cofinal exact subcategory closed under the duality.
We define the abelian group

GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q)

as the quotient monoid of the monoid of isometry classes of quadratic spaces in
\mathcal{U}
modulo the submonoid of isometry classes of quadratic spaces in
\mathcal{A}
.
The abelian monoid
GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q)
is indeed a group since for any quadratic space
(X,q)
in
\mathcal{U}
, the quadratic space
(X,q)\perp(X,q)\perp HY
lies in
\mathcal{A}
whenever
X\oplus Y\in\mathcal{A}
.
In particular,

-[X,q]=[X,q]+[HY]\in GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q).

Note that the group
GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q)
is independent of the exact structure of
\mathcal{U}
.
Similarly, one defies the abelian group
K_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
as the quotient abelian monoid of the monoid of isomorphism classes of objects in
\mathcal{U}
modulo the submonoid of isomorphism classes in
\mathcal{A}
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
By
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.27 and Theorem 6.5]
, the group
GW_{0}(\mathscr{E},Q)=\pi_{0}GW(\mathscr{E},Q)
is the abelian group generated by isometry classes
[X]
of quadratic spaces in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
modulo the relations
[X\perp Y]=[X]+[Y]
and
[M]=[H(L)]
for a metabolic space
M
in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
with Lagrangian
L
.

Let
(M,\xi)
be a metabolic space in
(\mathcal{U},Q)
with Lagrangian
L
.
Choose
K\in\mathcal{U}
such that
K\oplus L\in\mathcal{A}
.
Then
M\oplus H(K)\in\mathcal{A}
since it is an extension of
L\oplus K
and
(L\oplus K)^{\sharp}
both of which are in
\mathcal{A}
.
In
GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q)
we therefore have

[M,\xi]=-[H(K)]=[H(L)]
which shows that the map
GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},Q)\to GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q):[M,\xi]\mapsto[M,\xi]
in the following lemma is well-defined.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
Let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be a cofinal exact subcategory closed under the duality.
Then the following sequence of abelian groups is exact

GW_{0}(\mathcal{A},Q)\to GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},Q)\to GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q)\to 0

Proof.

Let
B
be the cokernel of the first map.
Since the composition is zero, we have a natural map
B\to GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q):[M]\mapsto[M]
.
But the map
GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q)\to B:[M]\to[M]
is clearly well-defined and an inverse to the map above.
∎

The left map in the lemma is also injective.
This is a special case of the following.

Theorem 4.2

(Cofinality for exact form categories)
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
Let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be a cofinal exact subcategory closed under the duality.
Then the inclusion
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
induces an isomorphism

GW_{i}(\mathcal{A},Q)\cong GW_{i}(\mathcal{U},Q)\hskip 12.91663pt\text{for }i\geq 1

and a monomorphism

GW_{0}(\mathcal{A},Q)\rightarrowtail GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},Q)

in degree
0
.

Proof.

In
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.15]
, the group completion of
i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)
of the category of quadratic spaces in an exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)

was denoted
GW^{\oplus}(\mathscr{E},Q)
.
We will use that if the realisation of a simplicial commutative
H
-space is group complete then group completing degree-wise that simplicial
H
-space doesn’t change the homotopy type of the realisation.
In particular, group completing degree-wise the commutative square of simplicial categories

\textstyle{i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{A},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{U},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{U}}

yields (after topological realisation) the homotopy equivalent square which is the (topological realisation of the) left square of the commutative diagram of simplicial spaces

\textstyle{GW^{\oplus}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{A},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{GW^{\oplus}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{U},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{GW_{0}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{U},\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{A},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\oplus}(S_{\bullet}\mathcal{A})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\oplus}(S_{\bullet}\mathcal{U})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K_{0}(S_{\bullet}\mathcal{U},S_{\bullet}\mathcal{A})}

in which the rows are homotopy fibrations (degree-wise and after realisation), by Cofinality for
GW^{\oplus}

[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.18]
and its
K
-theoretical analogue.
By Additivity (Corollary
3.3
) for
GW_{0}
and
K_{0}
, the right vertical map is a surjective map of simplicial abelian groups with kernel the constant simplicial abelian group
GW_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A},Q)
.
Taking vertical homotopy fibres of the map of homotopy fibrations yields the result.
∎

Example 4.3

(Idempotent and semi-idempotent competion)
.

An exact category is called idempotent complete if every idempotent has a kernel.
The idempotent completion
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
of an exact category with duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can})
is canonically an exact category with duality such that
\mathscr{E}\subset\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
is cofinal
[
Sch10a
, §5.1]
.
Recall that an object of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
is a pair
(A,p)
with
A\in\mathscr{E}
and
p^{2}=p:A\to A
an idempotent.
Arrows
f:(A,p)\to(B,q)
in
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
are arrows
f:A\to B
such that
f=fp=qf
.
Composition is composition in
\mathscr{E}
.
We have a fully faithful embedding
\mathscr{E}\subset\tilde{\mathscr{E}}:A\mapsto(A,1)
.
A sequence in
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
is called exact if it is a retract of an exact sequence in
\mathscr{E}
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category.
We extent
Q
to
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
as follows.
For an object
(A,p)
of
\tilde{E}
,
the Mackey functor of quadratic forms on
(A,p)
is the image of the Mackey functor of
A
under the idempotent induced by
p
.
For instance
Q(A,p)=\operatorname{Im}(Q(p):Q(A)\to Q(A))
.
By the Cofinality Theorem
4.2
, we have

GW_{i}(\mathscr{E},Q)\cong GW_{i}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}},Q),\hskip 8.61108pti\geq 1,\hskip 8.61108ptGW_{0}(\mathscr{E},Q)\subset GW_{0}(\tilde{\mathscr{E}},Q).

An exact category is called
semi-idempotent complete
if every arrow which has a section also has a kernel.
The
semi-idempotent completion
of
\mathscr{E}
is the full exact subcategory
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{0}
of
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
of objects
X\in\tilde{E}
for which there is
E\in\mathscr{E}
such that
X\oplus E
is isomorphic to an object of
\mathscr{E}
.
A sequence in
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{0}
is exact if it is exact in
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
.
The inclusion
\mathscr{E}\subset\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{0}
is cofinal and the Cofinality Theorem yields an equivalence of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

GW(\mathscr{E},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{0},Q)

since
(X,\xi)\perp HA
is in
\mathscr{E}
whenever
A,X\oplus A\in E
.

In a semi-idempotent complete category, if
fg
as an admissible monomorphism, then so is
g
, and if
fg
is an admissible epimorphism, then so is
f

[
Kel90
, Appendix A]
.

5.
The filtering localization theorem

Recall from
[
Sch04
]
that a fully exact inclusion
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
of exact categories is called
s-filtering

if

(1)

every map
A\to U
with
A\in\mathcal{A}
factors through an admissible subobject of

U
belonging to
\mathcal{A}
,

(2)

every map
U\to A
with
A
in
\mathcal{A}
factors through an admissible quotient
object of
U
belonging to
\mathcal{A}
,

(3)

for every deflation
U\twoheadrightarrow A
in
\mathcal{U}

with
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, there is an inflation
B\rightarrowtail U
with
B\in\mathcal{A}

such that the composition
B\to A
is a deflation in
\mathcal{A}
, and

(4)

for every inflation
A\rightarrowtail U
in
\mathcal{U}
with
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, there is a
deflation
U\twoheadrightarrow B
with
B\in\mathcal{A}
such that the composition

A\to B
is an inflation in
\mathcal{A}
.

Items (
1
) and (
2
) imply that

\mathcal{A}
is closed under admissible sub-objects and quotient-objects
in
\mathcal{U}
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be an s-filtering inclusion of exact
categories.
To simplify we will assume that
\mathcal{A}
is idempotent complete.
A
weak isomorphism

[
Sch04
, Definition 1.12]
is a finite composition of deflations with kernel in

\mathcal{A}
and inflations with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
.
By definition, weak isomorphisms are closed under composition.
They satisfy a calculus of fractions
[
Sch04
, Lemma 1.13]
.
In diagrams, weak isomorphisms are displayed as
\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\to}}
,
inflations with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
as
\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\rightarrowtail}}
, and
deflations with kernel in
\mathcal{A}
as
\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}
.
By
[
Sch04
, Proposition 1.16]
, the exact quotient category

\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
exists and is obtained from
\mathcal{U}
by formally inverting
the set of weak isomorphisms.
A sequence of
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
is a conflation in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
if and only if it is
isomorphic to the image of a conflation of
\mathcal{U}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be an
s
-filtering inclusion of exact categories with
\mathcal{A}
idempotent complete.
Then the following holds.

(1)

A map in
\mathcal{U}
is a weak isomorphism if and only if it is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.

(2)

For every weak isomorphism
f:X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}Y
in
\mathcal{U}
there is an inflation
s:U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}X
with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
such that
fs
is an inflation with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
.

(3)

For any admissible monomorphisms
u:X\rightarrowtail Y
and
s:Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y
in
\mathcal{U}
with
\operatorname{coker}(s)
in
\mathcal{A}
there are admissible monomorphisms
t:W\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}X
and
v:W\rightarrowtail Z
in
\mathcal{U}
with
\operatorname{coker}(t)
in
\mathcal{A}
such that
ut=sv
.

(4)

If in a map
f=(f_{0},f_{1},f_{2})
of admissible exact sequences in
\mathcal{U}
two of the three maps are weak isomorphisms then so is the third.

(5)

For every admissible monomorphism
v:V_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}V
in
\mathcal{U}
with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
and every admissible epimorphism
g:V\twoheadrightarrow U
in
\mathcal{U}
there is an admissible epimorphism
g_{0}:V_{0}\twoheadrightarrow U_{0}
and weak isomorphism
u:U_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U
in
\mathcal{U}
such that
gv=ug_{0}\in\mathcal{U}(V_{0},U)
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a map in
U
such that there is a map
g:Y\to Z
with
gf
an inflation in
\mathcal{U}
. If either
X
is in
\mathcal{A}
or
f
is a weak isomorphism, then
f
is an inflation.

Proof.

Part (
1
) is
[
Sch04
, Lemma 1.17 (6)]
.
Part (
2
) is
[
Sch04
, Lemma 1.17 (3)]
.
Part (
3
) is
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.5 (e)]
.
Part (
4
) is
[
Sch04
, Lemma 1.17 (7)]
.
Part (
5
) follows from (
3
) and (
4
).
Part (
5
) is
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.5 (g)]
.
∎

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
be an exact form category, and let

\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be a duality preserving s-filtering inclusion
of exact categories.
Assume
\mathcal{A}
is idempotent complete and closed under the duality functor
\sharp
.
Then
(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{A}})
is an exact form category and the inclusion

I:\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
defines a fully faithful form functor

(I,1,1):(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\subset(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{U}})

where
Q_{\mathcal{A}}
is the restriction of
Q_{\mathcal{U}}
to
\mathcal{A}
.
The duality
(\sharp,\operatorname{can})
on
\mathcal{U}
induces an exact duality and a natural double dual
identification on the quotient
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
, and we
consider
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
equipped with this structure of an exact category with
duality.
For an object
X
of
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
, we define the abelian group
Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X)
of forms on
X
as

Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X)=\operatorname{colim}_{Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}X}Q_{\mathcal{U}}(Y)

where the indexing category of the colimit has objects the weak isomorphisms with target
X
and morphisms the commutative triangles in
\mathcal{U}
.
The indexing category is filtering.
For an arrow
[gs^{-1}]:X\to Y
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
represented by the
\mathcal{U}
-diagram
gs^{-1}:X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\leftarrow}}X_{0}\to Y
with
s:X_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}X
a weak isomorphism and
[\xi,r]\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Y)
represented by
\xi\in Q(U)
for some weak isomorphism
r:U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}Y
, choose a weak isomorphism
r_{0}:U_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}X_{0}
and a
\mathcal{U}
-arrow
g_{0}:U_{0}\to U
such that
rg_{0}=gr_{0}
and set

[gs^{-1}]^{\bullet}\left([\xi,r]\right)=[g_{0}^{\bullet}(\xi),sr_{0}]\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X).

One checks that this is independent of the choices and makes
Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}
into a functor from
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})^{op}
to abelian groups.

Since
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}(X,X^{\sharp})=\operatorname{colim}_{Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}X}\mathcal{U}(Y,Y^{\sharp})
, the Mackey functor of quadratic forms in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}

\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}(X,X^{\sharp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}(X,X^{\sharp})

is the colimit

\operatorname{colim}_{Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}X}\left(\mathcal{U}(Y,Y^{\sharp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{U}}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{U}(Y,Y^{\sharp})\right).

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume that
\mathcal{A}
is idempotent complete.
Then the following tuple defines an exact form category:

(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}).

Proof.

We already know that
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can})
is an exact category with duality
[
Sch10a
, §8.2]
and it is clear from the definition that the conditions in Definition
2.1
(
1
) and (
2
) hold.
So we are left with checking condition (
3
) of Definition
2.1
.
Since every admissible exact sequence in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
is isomorphic to the image of an admissible exact sequence of
\mathcal{U}
we only need to check the condition in Definition
2.1
(
3
) for such sequences.
So, let
X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}Z
be an admissible exact sequence in
\mathcal{U}
and consider the sequence

0\to Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Z)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i^{\bullet},\rho(\phantom{\xi})\circ i)}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X)\times\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}(Y,X^{\sharp})

which we need to show is exact.

Injectivity of
p^{\bullet}
follows from Lemma
5.1
(
5
).
Let
[\xi,t]\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Z)
be represented by
\xi\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(U)
for some admissible monomorphism
t:U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Z
with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
(Lemma
5.1
(
2
)) such that
p^{\bullet}[\xi,t]=0\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Y)
.
Since
p^{\bullet}[\xi,t]=[g^{\bullet}\xi,t_{0}]
is represented by
g^{\bullet}(\xi)\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(V)
for
t_{0}:V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y
the pull-back of
t
along
p
and
g:V\twoheadrightarrow U
the induced admissible epimorphism, there is an admissible monomorphism
v:V_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}V

such that
v^{\bullet}g^{\bullet}(\xi)=0\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(V_{0})
.
By Lemma
5.1
(
5
), there are an admissible epimorphism
g_{0}:V_{0}\twoheadrightarrow U_{0}
and a weak isomorphism
u:U_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}U
such that

gv=ug_{0}\in\mathcal{U}(V_{0},U)
.
Then
0=v^{\bullet}g^{\bullet}(\xi)=g_{0}^{\bullet}u^{\bullet}(\xi)\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(V_{0})
implies

0=u^{\bullet}(\xi)\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(U_{0})
since
g_{0}^{\bullet}
is injective.
Then
[\xi,t]=[u^{\bullet}(\xi),tu]=0\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Z)
.

For exactness at
Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Y)
, let
[\xi,t]\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Y)
be represented by
\xi\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(U)
and the admissible monomorphism
t:U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}Y
in
\mathcal{U}
with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
.
Assume that
i^{\bullet}[\xi,t]=0
and
\rho[\xi,t]\circ i=0
.
Using Lemma
5.1
(
3
) we find
admissible monomorphisms
t_{0}:V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}X
and
j:V\rightarrowtail U
with
\operatorname{coker}t_{0}
in
\mathcal{A}
and
it_{0}=tj\in\mathcal{U}(V,Y)
such that
j^{\bullet}(\xi)=0\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(V)
and
\rho(\xi)j=0
.
Denote by
r:U\to U/V=\operatorname{coker}(j)
the quotient map and by
t_{1}:U/V\to Z
the induced map on quotients.
By Lemma
5.1
(
4
),
t_{1}
is a weak isomorphism.
By quadratic left-exactness of
Q_{\mathcal{U}}
, there is a unique
\zeta\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(U/V)
such that

r^{\bullet}(\zeta)=\xi
.
Then
[\zeta,t_{1}]\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Z)
and
p^{\bullet}[\zeta,t_{1}]=[\xi,t]\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(Y)
.
∎

We have the sequence exact form functors

(5.1)

(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{U}})\longrightarrow(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})

where the first is the canonical full inclusion,
Q_{\mathcal{A}}
is the restriction of
Q_{\mathcal{U}}
to
\mathcal{A}
, and the second form functor is the localisation functor
which on quadratic forms is the map

Q_{\mathcal{U}}(X)\longrightarrow Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X):\xi\mapsto[\xi,1_{X}].

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of the following theorem and its Grothendieck-Witt space version in Theorem
5.13
below.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
be an exact form category with strong duality, and
let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be an s-filtering inclusion of exact
categories closed under the duality.
Assume that
\mathcal{A}
is idempotent complete.
Then the sequence (
5.1
) induces a homotopy fibration on
classifying spaces of

\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}}){\longrightarrow}\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}).

Lemma 5.4
.

Assume the hypothesis of Theorem
5.3
.
Let
(X,\xi)
be a form in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
with
\rho(\xi)
a weak isomorphism which is
metabolic in
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
with Lagrangian the admissible monomorphism
L_{0}\subset X
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
Then there is a weak isomorphism of admissible exact sequences
in
\mathcal{U}
of the form

(5.2)

\textstyle{L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{l}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{\rho(\xi)}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{X/L\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\scriptstyle{l^{\sharp}\operatorname{can}}
\textstyle{(X/L)^{\sharp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 9.04166ptp^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{X^{\sharp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{L^{\sharp}}

such that
i^{\bullet}(\xi)=0\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(L)
and
L
is isomorphic to
L_{0}
as subobjects of
X
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
Moreover, there is a metabolic space
(Y,\zeta)
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
with Lagrangian
M
and an isometry
(X,\xi)\cong(Y,\zeta)
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
which takes
L
to
M
.

Proof.

For the underlying symmetric form
\rho(\xi)
, the diagram in the lemma was constructed in
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.8 (b)]
.
For this diagram we have
i^{\bullet}(\xi)=0
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
In view of the calculus of fractions and Lemma
5.1
(
2
), we can replace
L
with an admissible subobject in
\mathcal{U}
which is isomorphic to
L
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
such that
i^{\bullet}(\xi)=0
.

To construct the metabolic
Y
in
\mathcal{U}
, consider the quadratic form

\nu=(1\ 0)^{\bullet}(\xi)+\tau\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&p^{\sharp}\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}\left(X\oplus(X/L)^{\sharp}\right)

on
X\oplus(X/L)^{\sharp}
. Denote by
Y
the cokernel of the admissible monomorphism

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-i\\
l\end{smallmatrix}\right):L\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{\hbox{\kern 10.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces X\oplus(X/L)^{\sharp}

in
\mathcal{U}
and let
q:X\oplus(X/L)^{\sharp}\twoheadrightarrow Y
be the corresponding quotient map.
Note that

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\nu_{|L}&=&\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-i\\
l\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\bullet}\left((1\ 0)^{\bullet}(\xi)+\tau\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&p^{\sharp}\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\\
&=&(-i)^{\bullet}(\xi)+\tau\left((-i^{\sharp}\ l^{\sharp})\circ\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&p^{\sharp}\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\circ\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-i\\
l\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\\
&=&0\end{array}

and

(-i^{\sharp}\ l^{\sharp})\circ\rho(\nu)=(-i^{\sharp}\ l^{\sharp})\circ\left(\begin{matrix}\rho(\xi)&p^{\sharp}\\
\operatorname{can}\circ p&0\end{matrix}\right)=0

and that therefore there is a unique quadratic form
\zeta\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(Y)
on
Y
such that
q^{\bullet}(\zeta)=\nu
.
The form
\zeta
is non-degenerate since
\rho(\zeta)
is the canonical isomorphism from the push-out of the upper left corner of (
5.2
) to the pull-back of the lower right corner of (
5.2
).
In particular, we have an admissible exact sequence

(X/L)^{\sharp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j}}{{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{\hbox{\kern 10.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho(\zeta)\circ j^{\sharp}}}{{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 27.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}}(X/L)^{\sharp\sharp}

where

j=q\circ\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\hskip 12.91663pt\text{and}\hskip 12.91663ptj^{\bullet}(\zeta)=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\bullet}(\nu)=0

which shows that
(Y,\zeta)
is metabolic in
(\mathcal{U},Q)
with Lagrangian
j
.
Finally, in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
, the map
l
is an isomorphism and thus, the canonical map from
X
to the push-out of the left upper corner of (
5.2
) is an isomorphism and we can choose
M=(X/L)^{\sharp}
.
∎

Recall
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.27]
that the Witt group
W(\mathscr{E},Q)
of an exact form category with strong duality is the abelian monoid of isometry classes of quadratic spaces in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
under orthogonal sum modulo the submonoid of metabolic spaces.

W(\mathscr{E},Q)
is an abelian group since
(X,\xi)\perp(X,-\xi)
is metabolic for any quadratic space
(X,\xi)
.
By
[
Sch21
, Lemma 6.4]
, we have an isomorphism

\pi_{0}\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)\cong W(\mathscr{E},Q):[X,\xi]\mapsto[X,\xi].

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume the hypothesis of Theorem
5.3
.
Then the following sequence of abelian groups is exact

W(\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\to W(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})\to W(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}).

Proof.

The composition is clearly zero.
Let
(X,\xi)
be a quadratic space in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
which is stably metabolic in

\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
Then there is a metabolic space
(M,\mu)
in
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
such that
(X,\xi)\perp(M,\mu)
is metabolic in
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
.
By definition of
Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}
, we can assume

(M,\mu)
to be a form in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
with
\rho(\mu)
a weak isomorphism.
The quadratic form
(M,-\mu)
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
is also metabolic in
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
with same Lagrangian as
(M,\mu)
.
The orthogonal sum of forms

(X,\xi)\perp(M,\mu)\perp(M,-\mu)
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
is therefore metabolic in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
Consider the map of forms in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})

(5.3)

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&1\\
\rho(\mu)&0\end{smallmatrix}\right):(M,-\mu)\perp(M,\mu)\longrightarrow H^{\mu}(M)=(M\oplus M^{\sharp},p_{M}^{\bullet}(\mu)+h_{M})

where
h_{M}=\tau(p_{M}^{\sharp}\circ p_{M^{\sharp}})
is the hyperbolic form and

p_{M}:M\oplus M^{\sharp}\to M
,
p_{M^{\sharp}}:M\oplus M^{\sharp}\to M^{\sharp}
are the canonical projections.
This map does indeed preserve forms because

\begin{array}[]{rl}&\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&1\\
\rho(\mu)&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\bullet}\left(p_{M}^{\bullet}(\mu)+h_{M}\right)\\
=&\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&1\\
\rho(\mu)&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)^{\bullet}\left((1\ 0)^{\bullet}(\mu)+\tau\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\\
=&(-1\ 1)^{\bullet}(\mu)+\tau\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&\rho(\mu)^{\sharp}\\
1&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&1\\
\rho(\mu)&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\\
=&(1\ 0)^{\bullet}(-1)^{\bullet}(\mu)+(0\ 1)^{\bullet}(\mu)+\tau\left(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\rho(\mu)(-1\ 0)\right)+\tau\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-\rho(\mu)&0\\
\rho(\mu)&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\\
=&(1\ 0)^{\bullet}(-1)^{\bullet}(\mu)+(0\ 1)^{\bullet}(\mu)+\tau\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-\rho(\mu)&0\\
0&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\\
=&(1\ 0)^{\bullet}(-\mu)+(0\ 1)^{\bullet}(\mu)\end{array}

since
(-1)^{\bullet}=-1+\tau\rho
; see
[
Sch21
, Remark 2.3]
.
Note that
H^{\mu}(M)
is a quadratic space in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
with associated symmetric bilinear form

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\rho(\mu)&1\\
\operatorname{can}&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
As a weak isomorphism, the map (
5.3
) of forms defines an isometry in
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
.
It follows that the quadratic space

(Y,\psi)=(X,\xi)\perp H^{\mu}(M)
in
(\mathcal{U},Q)
is a metabolic in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
By Lemma
5.4
there is an
admissible monomorphism
i:L\rightarrowtail Y
in
\mathcal{U}
such that
i^{\bullet}(\psi)=0\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(L)

and such that the canonical map
L\to(Y/L)^{\sharp}\cong L^{\perp}
is a weak isomorphism.
By
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.5 (g)]
, the map
L\to L^{\perp}
is an admissible monomorphism in
\mathcal{U}
with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
.
In particular,
L
is a sublagrangian of
(Y,\psi)
, and the induced quadratic form
\zeta
on
L^{\perp}/L\in\mathcal{A}
is non-degenerate
[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.30]
.
Since
H^{\mu}(M)
is metabolic in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
,
[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.30]
implies
the equalities

[X,\xi]=[Y,\psi]=[L^{\perp}/L,\zeta]

in
W(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
.
This shows that
[X,\xi]
is in the image of
the map
W(\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\to W(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
.
∎

For an exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
, let
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathscr{E})\subset\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)
be the
full subcategory of metabolic spaces in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
.
Under the hypothesis of Theorem
5.3
, let

\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})\subset\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
be the full subcategory of those
quadratic spaces
(X,\xi)
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
for which there is a map

(A,\alpha)\to(X,\xi)
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
with
A
in
\mathcal{A}
.
Note that we have inclusions

(5.4)

\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U})\subset\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})\subset\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}}).

Lemma 5.6
.

On homotopy groups, the inclusion
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
and the two inclusions in (
5.4
) induce monomorphisms on
\pi_{0}
and isomorphisms on
\pi_{i}
for
i\geq 1
.

Proof.

The proof is
mutatis mutandis
the same as
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.10]
.
∎

The functor
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})\to\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
maps
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
into the
subcategory
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
of metabolic objects of
(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}})
and thus defines a functor

\lambda:\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}).

In fact,
