\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
is precisely the full subcategory of
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q)
of those
objects which have image in
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
in view of Lemma
5.4
and
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.5 (g)]
and
[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.30]
.
Theorem
5.3
follows from Lemmas
5.5
,

5.6
and the following proposition the proof of which will
occupy the rest of the section.

Proposition 5.7
.

Assume the hypothesis of Theorem
5.3
.
Then the sequence (
5.1
) induces a homotopy fibration on
classifying spaces of

\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\lambda}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}).

Proof.

We will write
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
for
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{A}})
and similarly for
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
In order to identify the homotopy fibre of
\lambda
, we will use Theorem B of
Quillen.
We will show that for a map
g:M\to N
in
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
, the induced
functor
g^{*}:(N\downarrow\lambda)\to(M\downarrow\lambda)
is a
homotopy equivalence on classifying spaces.
Since every object in
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
is metabolic, every object
is the target of a map from
0
.
Thus it suffices to show that
g^{*}
is a homotopy equivalence for

g:0\to N
.
Recall that the category
(N\downarrow\lambda)
has objects pairs

(X,\alpha)
with
X
an object of
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
and
\alpha:N\to\lambda X
a map in
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
.
Maps from
(X,\alpha)
to
(Y,\beta)
correspond to maps
s:X\to Y
in

\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
such that
\beta=\lambda(s)\circ\alpha
.
Composition is composition of the maps
s
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
.

Consider the following diagram of categories and functors

(5.5)

\textstyle{E_{g}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-9.04166ptp}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}
\textstyle{(N{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{N}}
\textstyle{(N{\downarrow}\lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 1.50694ptg^{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(0{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{0}}
\textstyle{(0{\downarrow}\lambda),}

where the categories and functors are as follows.

•

The functor
i_{N}
is the inclusion
of the full subcategory
(N{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)
of
(N{\downarrow}\lambda)

whose objects are those pairs

(X,\alpha)
for which
\alpha:N\to\lambda(X)
is an isomorphism.
Note that
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})=(0{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)
since
\mathcal{A}
is closed under subquotients in
\mathcal{U}
and its objects are precisely those of
\mathcal{U}
which are isomorphic to
0
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
Note that the categories
(N{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)
are non-empty, by Lemma
5.4
.

•

The category
E_{g}
has objects the pairs
(a,\alpha)
with

a:A\to X
a map in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
,
A\in\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
, and
\alpha:N\to\lambda(X)
an isomorphism in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
such that

\alpha\circ g=\lambda(a)
where
\lambda(A)
is identified with
0
via the
unique isomorphism
0\to\lambda(A)
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
.
A map
(a,\alpha)\to(b:B\to Y,\beta)
is a pair of maps
c:A\to B

and
s:X\to Y
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
such that
sa=bc
and
\beta=\lambda(s)\alpha
.
Composition is composition of the individual maps of the pairs.

•

The functor
p:E_{g}\to(N{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)

sends
(a:A\to X,\alpha)
to
(X,\alpha)
.

•

The functor
\gamma:E_{g}\to\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
sends

(a:A\to X,\alpha)
to
A
.

There is a natural transformation of functors
z

i_{0}\circ\gamma\to g^{*}\circ i_{N}\circ p
z
given by

i_{0}\circ\gamma(a:A\to X,\alpha)=(A,0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\longrightarrow}}g^{*}\circ i_{N}\circ p(a,\alpha)=(X,\alpha\circ g).

Thus, on classifying spaces, diagram (
5.5
) commutes up to homotopy.
The proof of Proposition
5.7
is complete once we show that

i_{N}
(hence
i_{0}
),
p
and
\gamma
induce
homotopy equivalences on classifying spaces.
This will be done in Lemmas
5.8
,
5.9
and
5.10
below.
∎

We will repeatedly use the Sublagrangian Construction
[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.30]
.
Recall that a sublagrangian of a quadratic space
(X,\xi)
in an exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,Q)
is an admissible subobject
i:L\subset X
such that
i^{\bullet}(\xi)=0
and the map
L\to L^{\perp}=\ker(i^{\sharp}\rho(\xi))
of subobjects of
X
is an admissible monomorphism.
In this case, there is a unique non-degenerate form
\bar{\xi}\in Q(L^{\perp}/L)
on
L^{\perp}/L
such that
p^{\bullet}(\bar{\xi})=\xi_{|L^{\perp}}\in Q(L^{\perp})
where
p:L^{\perp}\to L^{\perp}/L
is the quotient map.
In particular, to give an arrow
Y\to X
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)
is the same as to give a sublagrangian
L\subset X
of
X
and an isometry
L^{\perp}/L\cong Y
.

Lemma 5.8
.

For every object
N
of
\mathcal{Q}_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
, the full inclusion

i_{N}:(N{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)\to(N\downarrow\lambda)

is a homotopy
equivalence on classifying spaces.

Proof.

The lemma follows from Theorem A of Quillen once we show that for all
objects
(X,\alpha)
of
(N\downarrow\lambda)
, the comma category

\mathcal{C}=(i_{N}\downarrow(X,\alpha))
is contractible.
The map
\alpha:N\to\lambda(X)
is represented by a totally isotropic subspace

L_{0}\subset X
of
X
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
together with an
isometry
L_{0}^{\perp}/L_{0}\cong N
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
By the calculus of fractions and
[
Sch10a
, Lemmas 8.5 (a) and (f)]

we can assume that
L_{0}\subset X
is actually a totally isotropic subspace of

X
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
.
This shows that
\mathcal{C}
is non-empty, as

L_{0}^{\perp}/L_{0}
together with the canonical map in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U})
to
X
and the
isometry
L_{0}^{\perp}/L_{0}\cong N
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
defines an object of
\mathcal{C}
.

The comma category
\mathcal{C}
is equivalent to the category
with objects the totally isotropic subspaces
L\subset X
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
such that

L
is isomorphic, as a subobject of
X
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
, to
L_{0}
.
There is a (unique) map
L\to L^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{C}
if
L^{\prime}\subset L
.
Note that in this case,
L^{\prime}\subset L
is an admissible monomorphism, by Lemma
5.1
(
5
).
Using the calculus of fractions for the set of weak isomorphisms and Lemma

[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.5 (a)]
, we see that

\mathcal{C}
is a filtering
category so that the classifying space of
\mathcal{C}
is contractible

[
Qui73
, §1 Corollary 2]
.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

The functor
p:E_{g}\to(N{\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt}\lambda)
is a homotopy
equivalence on classifying spaces.

Proof.

In view of Quillen’s Theorem A it suffices to show that for any object

(X,\alpha)
of
(N\hskip 1.72218pt\downarrow\hskip-3.44444pt\wr\hskip 3.44444pt\lambda)
, the comma
category
\mathcal{C}=(p\downarrow(X,\alpha))
is contractible.
An object of
\mathcal{C}
is a sequence

A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\to}}U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\to}}X

of composable maps
a
,
s
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
with
A\in\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})

and
s
an isomorphism in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
such that
\lambda(sa)=\alpha g
.
A map from
(a,s)
to
B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle b}}{{\to}}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle t}}{{\to}}X
is a pair
of maps
c:A\to B
,
r:U\to V
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
such that
ra=bc

and
s=tr
.
Composition is composition of commutative diagrams in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
.

Let
J:\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}
be
the full subcategory of those sequences which are of the form
A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\to}}X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1}}{{\to}}X
.
The functor
G:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
which sends an object
(a,s)
to
(s\circ a,id)

and a map
(c,r)
to
(c,id_{X})
is a homotopy inverse to
J
because

GJ=id
and because
(id,s):(a,s)\to(s\circ a,id)
defines a natural
transformation
id\to JG
.

The category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is non-empty and
filtered, and thus contractible, by exactly
the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
5.8
.
In detail, the map
\alpha g:0\to X
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
is represented by a
Lagrangian

L_{0}
of
X
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
which, by
[
Sch10a
, Lemmas 8.5 (a) and (f)]
we
can assume to be a totally isotropic subspace
L_{0}
of
X
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
such that
the quotient
L_{0}^{\perp}/L_{0}
is in
\mathcal{A}
.
The category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is equivalent to the category whose objects are totally
isotropic subspaces
L\subset X
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
such that
L
is isomorphic to
L_{0}

as subobjects of
X
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
Maps are inclusions of totally isotropic subspaces.
This category is filtered by the calculus of fractions and
[
Sch10a
, Lemmas 8.5 (a)]
.
∎

The following lemma
completes the proof of Proposition
5.7
and thus of Theorem

5.3
.

Lemma 5.10
.

The functor
\gamma:E_{g}\to\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
is a homotopy equivalence on
classifying spaces.

Proof.

Again, the lemma follows from Theorem A of Quillen, once we show that for all
objects
A
of
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
, the comma category
\mathcal{C}=(A\downarrow\gamma)
is
contractible.
The category
\mathcal{C}
has objects
(A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle b}}{{\to}}B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\to}}X,N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\to}}\lambda X)
with
b
,
u
maps
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
,
B\in\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
and
s
an isomorphism in

\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
such that
\lambda(u)=sg
.
A map from
(b,u,s)
to
(A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle c}}{{\to}}C\stackrel{{\scriptstyle v}}{{\to}}Y,t)
is a pair of maps
a:B\to C
and
w:X\to Y
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
such that
c=ab
,
va=wu
and
t=\lambda(w)s
.
Composition is composition of commutative diagrams in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
.

Let
J:\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{C}
be the
full subcategory of objects
(b,u,s)
with
b=id_{A}
.
The functor
G:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\prime}

which sends an object
(b,u,s)
to
(id,ub,s)
and a map
(a,w)
to

(id_{A},w)
is a homotopy inverse of
J
because
GJ=id
and because
the map
(b,id_{X}):(id_{A},ub,s)\to(b,u,s)
defines a natural
transformation of functors
JG\to id
.
We will show that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a non-empty filtered (projective) system, so that

\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
and hence
\mathcal{C}
will be contractible.

Omitting the information
b=id_{A}
, the objects of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
are pairs

(A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u}}{{\to}}X,N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\to}}\lambda X)
with
u
a map in

\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
and
s
an isomorphism in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
such that
\lambda(u)=sg
.
The category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is non-empty.
Indeed, by definition of
Q_{m}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
the map
g:0\to N
is represented by a Lagrangian
L\subset N
in
\mathcal{U}/A
.
By Lemma
5.4
, there is a metabolic space
Y
with Lagrangian
M
in
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
and an isometry
s:N\cong Y
in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
carrying
L
to
M
.
The Lagrangian
M
of
Y
defines a map
h:0\to Y
in
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}}(\mathcal{U})
.
Since
\lambda(A)=0
, the pair

(\hskip 2.15277ptA\stackrel{{\scriptstyle 1_{A}\perp h}}{{\longrightarrow}}A\perp Y,\hskip 4.30554ptN\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\longrightarrow}}\lambda(Y))

defines an object of
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
.
This shows that the category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is non-empty.
The fact that
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
is a filtered projective system is shown in Lemma
5.12
below.
∎

Let
(X,\xi)
be a quadratic space in an exact form category with strong duality
(\mathscr{E},Q)
.
A map
u=i_{!}\circ p^{!}:V\to X
in
\mathcal{Q}(\mathscr{E})
defines an arrow in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)
with target
(X,\xi)
if and only if
\ker(p)\subset X
is a sublagrangian for
\xi
with orthogonal the source of
p
.
In this case, there is a unique form on
V
, given by the Sublagrangian Construction
[
Sch21
, Lemma 2.30]
, such that
u:V\to X
is a map in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)
.
We may write
u^{\bullet}\xi\in Q(V)
for this unique non-degenerate form and it the restriction of
\xi
to
V
.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality, and let
(\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
as in Theorem
5.3
.

(1)

Consider a string of maps

(5.6)

Y\to V\to X

in Quillen’s
\mathcal{Q}(\mathscr{E})
.
Let
(X,\xi)
by a quadratic space in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
.
Assume that (
5.6
) defines a string of maps in the form
Q
-construction for symmetric forms with target
(X,\rho(\xi))

and that the composition
Y\to X
defines a map in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)
with target
(X,\xi)
.
Then (
5.6
) defines a string of maps in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)
with target
(X,\xi)

(2)

Let
u:A\to X
be an arrow in Quillen’s
\mathcal{Q}(\mathcal{U})
with
A\in\mathcal{A}
.
Let
\xi_{i}\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(X)
,
i=1,2
, be non-degenerate forms such that
u
defines a map in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
with target
(X,\xi_{i})
,
i=1,2
.
Assume that
\rho(\xi_{1})=\rho(\xi_{2})
,
\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X)
and
u^{\bullet}(\xi_{1})=u^{\bullet}(\xi_{2})\in Q_{\mathcal{A}}(A)
.
Then there is a factorisation of
u
as
u=sa
with
a:A\to Y
and
s:Y\to X
such that
a
and
s
define maps in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U},Q_{\mathcal{U}})
,
s
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
, and
s^{\bullet}(\xi_{1})=s^{\bullet}(\xi_{2})\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(Y)
.

Proof.

For (
1
), we notice that the maps
Y\to V\to X
are given by sublagrangians
L_{0}\subset L_{1}\subset X
where
L_{0}\subset X
is a sublagrangian for
\rho(\xi)
giving rise to the map
V\to X
and
L_{1}
is a sublagrangian of
\xi
giving rise to the map
Y\to X
.
Since
\xi_{|L_{1}}=0
, we have
\xi_{|L_{0}}=0
, and
Y\to V\to X
defines a string of maps in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathscr{E},Q)
with target
(X,\xi)
.

We are left with proving (
2
).
The map
u=j_{!}\circ p^{!}:A\to X
is given by a subobject
i:L\subset X
which is a sublagrangian for
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
where
j:L^{\perp}=\ker(i^{\sharp}\varphi)\rightarrowtail X
for
\varphi=\rho(\xi_{1})=\rho(\xi_{2})
and
p:L^{\perp}\twoheadrightarrow A
is the quotient map.
The forms
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
restrict to the same form on
L^{\perp}
since

j^{\bullet}(\xi_{1})=p^{\bullet}(u^{\bullet}\xi_{1})=p^{\bullet}(u^{\bullet}\xi_{2})=j^{\bullet}(\xi_{2})
.
We will construct a factorisation
j=m\circ n
with
n:L^{\perp}\rightarrowtail M
,
m:M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}X
admissible monomorphisms and
\operatorname{coker}(m)\in\mathcal{A}
such that
m^{\bullet}\xi_{1}=m^{\bullet}\xi_{2}
as then
M^{\perp}\subset L
will be a sublagrangian for
\xi_{1}
and
\xi_{2}
defining the required factorisation
A\to Y=M/M^{\perp}\to X
.

Since
\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}\in Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}(X)
, there is an admissible monomorphism
K\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}X
with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
such that
(\xi_{1})_{|K}=(\xi_{1})_{|K}\in Q_{\mathcal{U}}(K)
.
By Lemma
5.1
(
3
),
K
and
L^{\perp}
have a common admissible subobject
K_{0}
such that
K_{0}\rightarrowtail L^{\perp}
has cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
P
be the pushout of
K
and
L
over their subobject
K_{0}
.
The induced map
q:P\to X
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
and
q^{\bullet}(\xi_{1})=q^{\bullet}(\xi_{2})
in view of Lemma
2.10
.
The map on quotients
P/L^{\perp}\to X/L^{\perp}
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}
.
In view of Lemma
5.1
(
2
), there is an admissible monomorphism
N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}P/L^{\perp}
with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
such that the composition
N\to X/L^{\perp}
is also an admissible monomorphism with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
M
be the pull-back of
N\to X/L^{\perp}
along the admissible epimorphism
P\to P/L^{\perp}
.
Then the induced map
M\to X
is an admissible monomorphism with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
as a pullback of such,
(\xi_{1})_{|M}=(\xi_{2})_{|M}
as
M\to X
factors through
P
, and
M
contains
L^{\perp}
as admissible subobject.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

(1)

Let
u_{i}:A\to X_{i}
,
i=1,2
, be two maps in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U})

with
A
an object of
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
.
Assume that there is an isomorphism
s:\lambda(X_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\cong}}{{\to}}\lambda(X_{2})
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
such that
s\circ\lambda(u_{1})=\lambda(u_{2})
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
.
Then there are maps
u:A\to V
and
s_{i}:V\to X_{i}
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U})
such that

s_{i}u=u_{i}
,
s\circ\lambda(s_{1})=\lambda(s_{2})
, and such that
s_{i}
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
,
i=1,2
.

(2)

Let
x:A\to X
,
u_{i}:X\to Y
,
i=1,2
be maps in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U})
with
A
in

\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{A})
and
\lambda(u_{i})
isomorphisms in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
satisfying

u_{1}x=u_{2}x
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U})
and
\lambda(u_{1})=\lambda(u_{2})
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
.
Then there are maps
z:A\to Z
,
v:Z\to X
in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U})
such
that
\lambda(v)
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{Q}\operatorname{Form}(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A})
,
u_{1}v=u_{2}v
, and
x=vz
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
5.11
,
Lemma
5.12
follows from the symmetric forms case proved in
[
Sch10a
, Lemmas 8.16 and 8.17]
.
∎

Theorem 5.13
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality, and
let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be an s-filtering fully exact inclusion of exact
categories closed under the duality.
Assume that
\mathcal{A}
is idempotent complete.
Then the sequence (
5.1
) induces a homotopy fibration of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

GW(\mathcal{A},Q)\longrightarrow GW(\mathcal{U},Q){\longrightarrow}GW(\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A},Q_{\mathcal{U}/\mathcal{A}}).

Proof.

In view of the definition of the Grothendieck-Witt space, this follows from
Theorem
5.3
and its
K
-theory analog
[
Sch04
, Theorem
2.1]
.
∎

6.
The cone construction

In this section we generalise from symmetric forms to arbitrary forms the cone construction of
[
Sch10a
, §9]
.
Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category and
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be a fully exact subcategory closed under the duality.
We consider
\mathcal{A}
as an exact form category by restricting the functors
\sharp
and
Q
to
\mathcal{A}
.
In this section, we will construct an exact form category
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
together with a duality preserving fully faithful form functor

\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
such that the sequence
\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
induces a homotopy fibration of Grothendieck-Witt spaces.

6.1.
The form category
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category.
Let
\mathcal{N}
be the totally ordered set
\mathcal{N}=\mathbb{N}\sqcup\mathbb{N}^{op}
in which every element
of
\mathbb{N}^{op}
is greater than every element of
\mathbb{N}
and the set of non-negative intergers
\mathbb{N}
is equipped with its usual order.
I will write
0,1,2,...
for elements of
\mathbb{N}
and
0^{op},1^{op},2^{op},...
for elements in
\mathbb{N}^{op}
.
So
0<1<2<...<2^{op}<1^{op}<0^{op}
.
The poset
\mathcal{N}
is equipped with the strict duality
n\leftrightarrow n^{op}
.
As explained in §
2
and
[
Sch21
, Lemma 6.2]
, the functor category
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U})
is an exact form category.
For an object
U
of
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U})
we may write
U_{n}
for
U(n)
and
U^{n}
for
U(n^{op})
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be fully exact subcategory
closed under the duality.
Recall from
[
Sch10a
, §9.1]
the category
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
which is the full subcategory of the functor category
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U})
of those objects
U:\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{U}
such that

(1)

for all
n\leq m\in\mathbb{N}
, the maps
U_{n}\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\rightarrowtail}}U_{m}

and
U^{m}\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}U^{n}

are admissible monomorphisms with cokernel in
\mathcal{A}
and admissible epimorphisms with kernel in
\mathcal{A}
,
respectively, and

(2)

there is a
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the maps

U_{n}\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\rightarrowtail}}U^{n+k}
are admissible monomorphisms with cokernel in

\mathcal{A}
, and the maps
U_{n+k}\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}U^{n}

are admissible epimorphisms with kernel in
\mathcal{A}
.

The category
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
is closed under extensions and duality in
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U})
and is thus equipped with a structure of exact form category such that the inclusion
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})\subset\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{U})
preserves and reflects admissible exact sequences, duality and forms.
In particular, for an object
U
of
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
, the abelian group of forms
Q(U)
on
U
is the set of pairs
(\xi,\varphi)
where
\xi=(\xi_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a family of forms
\xi_{n}\in Q(U_{n})
such that
\xi_{n}=U(n\leq m)^{\bullet}\xi_{m}
for all
n\leq m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varphi:U\to U^{\sharp}
is a symmetric form such that
\rho(\xi_{n})=U(n<n^{op})^{\sharp}\circ\varphi_{n}
.
When
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{A}
, we may write
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{A})
in place of
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
.

6.2.
The cone form category
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})

For
k\in\mathbb{N}
, the functors
\varepsilon_{k}:\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{N}:n\mapsto n+k,\ n^{op}\mapsto n^{op}
and
\varepsilon^{k}:\mathcal{N}\to\mathcal{N}:n\mapsto n,\ n^{op}\mapsto(n+k)^{op}
together with the unique natural transformations
1\to\varepsilon_{k}
and
\varepsilon^{k}\to 1
induce exact functors
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}):U\mapsto U_{[k]}=U\circ\varepsilon_{k}
,
U\mapsto U^{[k]}=U\circ\varepsilon^{k}
and natural transformations
e_{[k]}:U\to U_{[k]}
and
e^{[k]}:U^{[k]}\to U
.
Note that
e_{[i]}\circ e_{[j]}=e_{[i+j]}
,
e^{[i]}\circ e^{[j]}=e^{[i+j]}
and
e_{[i]}\circ e^{[j]}=e^{[j]}\circ e_{[i]}
.
The category
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
is obtained from
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
by formally inverting the arrows
e_{[k]}
and
e^{[k]}
for all objects
U
of
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
.
In particular, the objects of
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
are the same as the objects of
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
, and for
U,V\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
, the set of arrows
U\to V
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
is

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(U,V)=\operatorname{colim}_{i,j}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U^{[i]},V_{[j]})

where we abbreviated
\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
and
\mathcal{C}_{0}=\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
.
The colimit is taken over the projective system

\cdots\to U^{[3]}\to U^{[2]}\to U^{[1]}\to U
and over the inductive system

V\to V_{[1]}\to V_{[2]}\to V_{[3]}\to\cdots
induced by the maps
e^{[1]}
and
e_{[1]}
.
Composition of maps is defined as follows.
If the maps
[f]:U\to V
and
[g]:V\to W
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})

are represented by the maps
f:U^{[i]}\to V_{[j]}
and
g:V^{[k]}\to W_{[l]}

in
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
, their composition
[g]\circ[f]
is represented by the map

g_{[j]}\circ f^{[k]}:U^{[i+k]}\to V^{[k]}_{[j]}\to W_{[l+j]}
.
By
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 9.2]
, the category
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
is an exact category with duality where a sequence is admissibly exact if it isomorphic in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
to the image of an admissible exact sequence of
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
under the functor
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
.
The duality functor is induced by that of
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
so that
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
preserves duality.
Since
(U_{[k]})^{\sharp}=(U^{\sharp})^{[k]}
,
(U^{[k]})^{\sharp}=(U^{\sharp})_{[k]}
,

e_{[k]}(U)^{\sharp}=e^{[k]}(U^{\sharp})
,
e^{[k]}(U)^{\sharp}=e_{[k]}(U^{\sharp})
we have

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(U,U^{\sharp})&=&\operatorname{colim}_{i,j}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U^{[i]},(U^{\sharp})_{[j]})\\
&=&\operatorname{colim}_{i,j}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U^{[i]},(U^{[j]})^{\sharp})\\
&=&\operatorname{colim}_{i}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U^{[i]},(U^{[i]})^{\sharp})\end{array}

where the last colimit is over the restrictions along
e^{[1]}
.
Thus, taking colimit over the restrictions along
e^{[1]}
of the functorial Mackey functors
(\tau,\rho)
on
\mathcal{C}_{0}(U,\mathcal{A})
defines a functorial Mackey functor

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(U,U^{\sharp})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{C}}(U)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(U,U^{\sharp})

on
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
where

Q_{\mathcal{C}}(U)=\operatorname{colim}_{i}Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U^{[i]})

is the colimit over the inductive system

Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e^{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U^{[1]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e^{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(U^{[2]})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e^{\bullet}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\cdots

where we abbreviated
e=e^{[1]}
.
Since a filtered colimit of left exact sequences is left exact, this defines the exact form category

(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}),\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{C}})
.
When
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{A}
, we may write
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
in place of
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
and call it the
cone category
of
\mathcal{A}
.

The constant diagram functor defines a duality preserving fully faithful exact form functor

(6.1)

c:(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)\longrightarrow(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}),\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{C}})

which is
s
-filtering
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 9.3]
.
In particular, the quotient exact form category
(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{S\mathcal{U}})
is defined; see Lemma
5.2
.
We may write
Q_{S\mathcal{U}}
for the induced quadratic functor
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{U}}
on the quotient
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{U}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category, and let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be a fully exact subcategory closed under the duality functor.
Assume that
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{U}
are idempotent complete.
Then the inclusion
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
induces an equivalence of exact form categories

\left(\frac{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})}{\mathcal{A}},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{S}\mathcal{A}}\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})}{\mathcal{U}},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{S}\mathcal{U}}\right).

Proof.

By
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 9.4]
, the functor
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})/\mathcal{U}
is a duality preserving equivalence of exact categories.
Thus we have show that it induces isomorphisms of abelian groups of forms, that is, for
A\in\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
we have to show that the map

Q_{\mathcal{S}\mathcal{A}}(A)\to Q_{\mathcal{S}\mathcal{U}}(A)

is an isomorphism.
This is the map

\underset{B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}A}{\operatorname{colim}}\ Q_{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})}(B)\longrightarrow\underset{U\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrowtail}}A}{\operatorname{colim}}\ Q_{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},A)}(U)

where the first colimit is taken over those admissible monomorphisms
B\rightarrowtail A
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
with cokernel isomorphic to an object in
\mathcal{A}
and the second colimit is taken over those admissible monomorphisms
U\rightarrowtail A
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
with cokernel isomorphic to an object in
\mathcal{U}
.
Since both indexing categories are left filtering posets, it suffices to show that for every object in the right indexing category, there is an object in the left indexing category that maps to it.

So, let
U\rightarrowtail A
be an object of the right indexing category, that is, an admissible monomorphism in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
with quotient a constant diagram
A/U=cV
with in
V\in\mathcal{U}
.
The quotient map
A\twoheadrightarrow cV
factors as
A\twoheadrightarrow cA^{n}\to cV
for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
where the first map is an admissible epimorphism in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})

[
Sch10a
, Proof of Lemma 9.3]
.
Since
\mathcal{U}
is idempotent complete, the map
A^{n}\to V
is an admissible epimorphism in
\mathcal{U}

[
Sch10a
, Lemma 8.5 (g)]
.
Let
B
be the kernel of
A\twoheadrightarrow cA^{n}
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
and let
W
be the kernel of
A^{n}\twoheadrightarrow V
in
\mathcal{U}
.
Then
B
is an admissible subobject of
U
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})
such that
U/B=cW\in\mathcal{U}
,
and the admissible monomorphism
B\rightarrowtail A
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})
has quotient
cA^{n}\in\mathcal{A}
.
In other words,
B\rightarrowtail A
is an object of the left indexing category and a subobject of
U\rightarrowtail A
.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality.
Then there is a non-singular exact form functor
(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi):\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}
and an
isometry of form functors

id\perp(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi)\cong(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi).

In particular, the
K
-theory and Grothendieck-Witt spaces of
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}
are contractible.

Proof.

In
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 9.5]
we have constructed an exact form functor
(T,\varphi):\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}
,
\varphi:T\sharp\cong\sharp T
and an isometry
1\perp(T,\varphi)\cong(T,\varphi)
of form functors of underlying exact categories with duality.
Here we need to extend
(T,\varphi)
to a form functor
(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi)
between form categories
such that the isometry above defines an isometry
1\perp(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi)\cong(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi)
respecting quadratic forms.

Recall
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 9.5]
the duality preserving functor
[-1]:\mathcal{C}_{0}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}_{0}\mathcal{A}
defined by
(A[-1])_{i}=A_{i-1}
,
(A[-1])^{i}=A^{i-1}
,
i\geq 1
, and
(A[-1])_{0}=(A[-1])^{0}=0
with the same structure maps as
A
(shifted by
1
) and zero elsewhere.
On quadratic forms, we define
[-1]
by

Q(A)\to Q(A[-1]):(\xi,\varphi)\mapsto(\xi[-1],\varphi[-1])

where
(\xi[-1])_{i}=\xi_{i-1}
,
(\varphi[-1])_{i}=\varphi_{i-1}
and zero elsewhere,
i\geq 1
.
Note that for all
\xi\in Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(A)
we have

(6.2)

e_{[1]}^{\bullet}(\xi)=(e^{[1]})^{\bullet}(\xi[-1])\in Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(A[-1]^{[1]})

where

\textstyle{A}
\textstyle{(A[-1])^{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-9.04166pte_{[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 6.02777pte^{[1]}}
\textstyle{A[-1].}

The form functor
(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi):\mathcal{C}_{0}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}_{0}\mathcal{A}
is the orthogonal sum

(T,\varphi_{q}^{0},\varphi)=\perp_{r\geq 0}[-1]^{r}

where
[-1]^{r}
is the
r
-th iteration of
[-1]
.
For instance,

(TA)_{i}=\bigoplus_{0\leq r\leq i}A_{r},\hskip 12.91663pt(TA)^{i}=\bigoplus_{0\leq r\leq i}A^{r},\hskip 12.91663pt(\varphi_{q}^{0}(\xi))_{i}=\perp_{0\leq r\leq i}\xi_{r}

with the understanding that
A_{i}
,
A^{i}
,
\xi_{i}
are zero when
i<0
.
By definition, we have an isometry
1\perp T[-1]\cong T
.

In
[
Sch10a
, Lemma 9.5]
we proved that
(T,\varphi)
defines a form functor
(C\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can})\to(\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can})
using a natural transformation

\alpha_{A}:(TA)^{[1]}\to T(A^{[1]})
making the following diagram commute

\textstyle{TA\hskip 8.61108pt}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hskip 8.61108ptT(A^{[1]})}
\scriptstyle{T(e^{[1]})}
\textstyle{(TA)^{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e^{[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{A}}
\textstyle{\hskip 17.22217pt(T(A^{[1]}))^{[1]}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(T(e^{[1]}))^{[1]}}
\scriptstyle{e^{[1]}}

We set
\alpha_{A}(0)=1_{TA}
,
\alpha_{A}(1)=\alpha_{A}
and define recursively the natural transformation

\alpha_{A}(n)=\alpha_{A^{[n-1]}}\circ(\alpha_{A}(n-1))^{[1]}:(TA)^{[n]}\to T(A^{[n]}).

The diagram then shows that
\alpha(i)\circ e=(Te)\circ\alpha(i+1)
, and we obtain the map
of colimits

\textstyle{\operatorname{colim}_{n}Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}(A^{[n]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi^{0}_{q}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{colim}Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}T(A^{[n]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{colim}(\alpha(n)^{\bullet})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{colim}Q_{\mathcal{C}_{0}}((TA)^{[n]})}

which defines
\varphi_{q}:Q_{\mathcal{C}}(A)\to Q_{\mathcal{C}}(TA)
and completes the definition of the form functor
(T,\varphi_{q},\varphi):\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}
.
In view of the equality (
6.2
), we have an isometry of form functors
e^{[1]}\circ(e_{[1]})^{-1}:1\to[-1]
which turns the isometry
1\perp T[-1]\cong T
into the isometry
1\perp T\cong T
of form functors
\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{C}\mathcal{A}
.
∎

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
(\mathcal{U},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with strong duality and let
\mathcal{A}\subset\mathcal{U}
be a fully exact subcategory closed under the duality.
Consider
\mathcal{A}
as an exact form category by restriction of
\sharp
and
Q
to
\mathcal{A}
.
Assume that
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{U}
are idempotent complete.
Then the commutative square

\textstyle{{\mathcal{A}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{U}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A})}

of exact form categories and fully faithful exact form functors
induces a homotopy cartesian square of
Grothendieck-Witt spaces with contractible lower left corner.

Proof.

By Theorem
5.13
,
taking vertical quotients yields a map of homotopy fibrations of Grothendieck-Witt spaces which is an equivalence on base spaces (Lemma
6.1
).
This proves that the square in the theorem is homotopy cartesian after taking Grothendieck-Witt spaces.
The lower left corner is contractible, by Lemma
6.2
.
∎

Part II
Form categories with weak equivalences

7.
Form categories with weak equivalences

Recall
[
Sch10b
]
that an
exact category with weak equivalences
is a pair
(\mathscr{E},w)

with
\mathscr{E}
an exact category and
w\subset\operatorname{Mor}\mathscr{E}
a set of
morphisms, called weak equivalences, which contains all identity morphisms, is
closed under isomorphisms, retracts, push-outs along admissible monomorphisms, pull-backs
along admissible epimorphisms, composition and the
2
out of three property for
composition (if
2
of the
3
maps among
a
,
b
,
ab
are in
w
then so is
the third).
A weak equivalence is usually depicted as
\stackrel{{{}_{\sim}}}{{\to}}
in diagrams.
A functor
F:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
between exact categories with weak equivalences

(\mathcal{A},w)
and
(\mathcal{B},w)
is called
exact
if it preserves admissible exact sequences and weak equivalences.
For an exact category with weak equivalences
(\mathscr{E},w)
, we will write
w\mathscr{E}
for
the subcategory of
\mathscr{E}
whose arrows are the weak equivalences.
An object
E
in
\mathscr{E}
is called
acyclic
if the map
0\to E
is a weak equivalence.
We denote by
\mathscr{E}^{w}\subset\mathscr{E}
the full subcategory of acyclic objects.
By the following lemma, the subcategory is closed under extensions in
\mathscr{E}
and thus inherits the structure of an exact category from
\mathscr{E}
in which a sequence is admissible exact in
\mathscr{E}^{w}
if and only if it is in
\mathscr{E}
.
For a refinement of the following Lemma; see Lemma
9.2
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w)
be an exact category with weak equivalences.
Then the following hold.
An admissible epimorphism in
\mathscr{E}
is a weak equivalence if and only if its kernel is acyclic.
An admissible monomorphism in
\mathscr{E}
is a weak equivalence if and only if its cokernel is acyclic.
If in a map of admissible exact sequences in
\mathscr{E}

(7.1)

\textstyle{X_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{-1}}
\textstyle{X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{Y_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{1}}

the maps
f_{-1}
and
f_{1}
are weak equivalences then so is
f_{0}
.

Proof.

Consider composible weak equivalences
f:X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}Y
,
g:Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\twoheadrightarrow}}Z
with
g
an admissible epimorphism.
Let
i:A\rightarrowtail Y
be the kernel of
g
.
Then
gf
is a weak equivalence, and we have the following pull-back square

\textstyle{A\oplus X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(0\ 1)}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{(i\ f)}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{gf}
\textstyle{Z}

The canonical inclusion
X\to A\oplus X
is a weak equivalence since its composition with the weak equivalence
(i\ f)
is the weak equivalence
f
.
As a retract of
X\to A\oplus X
, the map
0\to A
is a weak equivalence.
Applied to
f=id
, this shows that
\ker(g)
is weakly equivalent to
0
if
g
is an admissible epimorphism which is a weak equivalence.
Conversely, if
\ker(g)
is weakly equivalent to
0
and if
g
is an admissible epimorphism then
g
is the push-out of the weak equivalence
A\to 0
along the admissible monomorphism
A\to Y
.

The second statement is similar and we omit the details.

For the last statement, we simply remark that
f_{0}
factors as
X_{0}\to P\to Y_{0}
where

X_{0}\to P
is the push-out of the upper left corner of the diagram and
P\to Y_{0}
is the pull-back of the lower right corner.
As both maps are weak equivalences, so is their composition
f_{0}
.
∎

Definition 7.2
.

An
exact form category with weak equivalences
is a tuple

(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)

where
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can})
is an exact category with weak equivalences and duality
[
Sch10b
, Section 2.3]
and the quadruple
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
is an exact form category
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.21]
.
In particular,
\sharp:\mathscr{E}^{op}\to\mathscr{E}
is exact and preserves weak equivalences,
\operatorname{can}_{X}:X\to X^{\sharp\sharp}
is a natural weak equivalence, and
Q
is quadratic left exact, that is, sends admissible exact sequences to left exact sequences as in Definition
2.1
(
3
).

Example 7.3
.

Any exact form category
(\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
with strong duality defines an exact form category with weak equivalences
(\mathscr{E},i,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
with set of weak equivalences the isomorphisms in
\mathscr{E}
.

Recall from
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.7]
the category
\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)
of quadratic forms in (
\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
.
Its objects are pairs
(X,\xi)
where
X\in\mathscr{E}
is an object of
\mathscr{E}
and
\xi\in Q(X)
is a form on
X
.
Arrows
f:(X,\xi)\to(Y,\psi)
are those maps
f:X\to Y
in
\mathscr{E}
for which
\xi=f^{\bullet}(\psi)
.
Composition of arrows is composition in
\mathscr{E}
.

Definition 7.4

(Quadratic spaces)
.

A
quadratic space
in an exact form category with weak equivalences
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
is a form
(X,\xi)
in
(\mathscr{E},Q)
such that the associated symmetric form
\rho(\xi):X\to X^{\sharp}
is a weak equivalence.
We denote by

w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)

the category of quadratic spaces in
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
and arrows those maps in
\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)
which are weak equivalences in
(\mathscr{E},w)
.

Definition 7.5
.

A form functor
(F,\varphi_{q},\varphi):(\mathcal{A},w,*,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\to(\mathcal{B},w,*,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{B}})
of exact form categories with weak equivalences is called
non-singular
if the duality compatibility map
\varphi:F*\to*F
is a weak equivalence.
We will denote by

(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},w;\mathcal{B},w),\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)

the fully exact form subcategory of
(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}),\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
of Lemma
2.8
consisting of those functors that preserve weak equivalences.
Note that an exact form functor is non-singular if and only if it defines a quadratic space in the exact form category with weak equivalences
(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},w;\mathcal{B},w),w,\sharp\operatorname{can}Q)
where a map of exact functors
F\to G:(\mathcal{A},w)\to(\mathcal{B},w)
is a weak equivalence if
F(A)\to G(A)
is a weak equivalence for all objects
A
of
\mathcal{A}
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w)
be an exact category with weak equivalences.
Recall from
[
Wal85
, Section 1.3]
the simplicial exact category with weak equivalences
n\mapsto(S_{n}\mathscr{E},w)
and the

K
-theory space
K(\mathscr{E},w)=\Omega|wS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}|
of
(\mathscr{E},w)
.
If
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
is an exact form category with weak equivalences, the category

S_{n}\mathscr{E}
is equipped with the structure of exact category with weak equivalences and duality
(S_{n}\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can})

[
Sch10b
, Section 2.6]
and a compatible structure of exact
form category
(S_{n}\mathscr{E},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
(§
2
and
[
Sch21
, Section 6]
) making
(S_{n}\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
into an exact form category with weak equivalences.
The edge-wise subdivision
n\mapsto\mathcal{R}_{n}\mathscr{E}=S_{[n]^{op}[n]}\mathscr{E}=S_{2n+1}\mathscr{E}

defines the simplicial exact form category with weak equivalences

n\mapsto(\mathcal{R}_{n}\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q).

The associated categories of quadratic spaces define a simplicial category

n\mapsto w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{n}\mathscr{E},Q).

Definition 7.6

(Grothendieck-Witt space)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
The composition
w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)\to w\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}\to wS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}

of simplicial categories, in which
the first arrow is the forgetful functor
(X,\xi)\mapsto X
,
and the second is the canonical map
X^{e}_{\bullet}\to X_{\bullet}
of
simplicial objects induced by
[n]\subset[n]^{op}[n]:i\mapsto i

yields a map of simplicial categories

(7.2)

w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)\to wS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}

and thus a map on classifying spaces

(7.3)

|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)|\to|wS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}|

whose homotopy fibre (with respect to
a zero object of
\mathscr{E}
as base point of
wS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}
) is defined to be the

Grothendieck-Witt space

GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)

of
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
.
Thus, we have a homotopy fibration

GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\to|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)|\to|wS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}|.

Inclusion of degree-zero simplicies defines a map of simplicial categories

w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)=w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{0}\mathscr{E},Q)\to w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)

where the left simplicial category is simplicially constant.
The composition

(7.4)

w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)\to w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)\to wS_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}

factors through
wS_{0}\mathscr{E}=0
und we obtain
a universal map

(7.5)

|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q)|\to GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)

from the category of quadratic spaces to its Grothendieck-Witt space.
By the main theorem of
[
Sch21
]
, this map is a group completion if
w
is the set of isomorphisms and if every admissible exact sequence in
\mathscr{E}
splits.

Finally, we define the

higher Grothendieck-Witt groups
of
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
as the homotopy
groups

GW_{i}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)=\pi_{i}GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q),\phantom{1234}i\geq 0.

We give a presentation of the group
GW_{0}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)
in Theorem
8.4
below.
For the next lemma, recall from
[
Sch21
, Definition 2.7]
the definition of a natural transformation between form functors, that is, a map between quadratic spaces in the exact form category with weak equivalences
(\operatorname{Fun}_{ex}(\mathcal{A},w;\mathcal{B},w),w,\sharp\operatorname{can}Q)
.

Lemma 7.7
.

Let
f:(F,\varphi_{q},\varphi)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}(G,\psi_{q},\psi)
be a natural weak equivalence between non-singular exact form functors

(F,\varphi_{q},\varphi),(G,\psi_{q},\psi):(\mathcal{A},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{A}})\to(\mathcal{B},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q_{\mathcal{B}})

of exact form categories with weak equivalences.
Then on Grothendieck-Witt spaces,
(F,\varphi_{q},\varphi)
and
(G,\psi_{q},\psi)
induce homotopic maps

GW(F,\varphi_{q},\varphi)\sim GW(G,\psi_{q},\psi):GW(\mathcal{A},w,Q_{\mathcal{A}})\to GW(\mathcal{B},w,Q_{\mathcal{B}}).

Proof.

The natural weak equivalence
f
defines natural transformations of the two functors after applying the
w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{n},Q)
- and
wS_{\bullet}
-constructions and thus functors
[1]\times w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{n}\mathcal{A},\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{A}})\to w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{n}\mathcal{B},\mathcal{Q}_{\mathcal{B}})
and
[1]\times wS_{\bullet}\mathcal{A}\to wS_{\bullet}\mathcal{B}
compatible with the simplicial structure and forgetful maps.
This defines homotopies between the
w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet},Q)
.
wS_{\bullet}
-constructions and leads upon taking homotopy fibres
to the homotopy
I\times GW(\mathcal{A},w,Q_{\mathcal{A}})\to GW(\mathcal{B},w,Q_{\mathcal{B}})
between the maps induced by
(F,\varphi_{q},\varphi)
and
(G,\psi_{q},\psi)
.
∎

Definition 7.8

(Hyperbolic form category)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w)
be an exact category with weak equivalences.
Recall from
[
Sch10b
, Section 2.9]
the hyperbolic exact category with weak equivalences and duality
(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)
.
As exact category with weak equivalences it is
(\mathscr{E}\times\mathscr{E}^{op},w\times w^{op})
, and it has a strict duality
(X,Y)^{\sharp}=(Y,X)
,
(f,g)^{\sharp}=(g,f)
.
We consider
\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}
as an exact form category with functor of quadratic forms the symmetric forms.
Thus,
Q(X,Y)=\mathscr{E}(X,Y)
and the functorial Mackey-functor of forms is

\textstyle{\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}[(X,Y),(Y,X)]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 15.06943pt(f,g)\mapsto f+g}
\textstyle{Q(X,Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-18.08331ptf\mapsto(f,f)}
\textstyle{\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E}[(X,Y),(Y,X)].}

In
[
Sch10b
, Proposition 1]
we constructed a homotopy equivalence

(7.6)

GW(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}K(\mathscr{E},w)

which makes the following diagram commute

\textstyle{w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{GW(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{w\mathscr{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K(\mathscr{E},w)}

where the horizontal maps are the universal maps and the left vertical map is the weak equivalence
(X,Y,f)\mapsto X
of
[
Sch10b
, Lemma 3]
.

Let
(E,w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
The
hyperbolic
and
forgetful
form functors of §
3
preserve weak equivalences and define the sequence of non-singular exact form functors

(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w).

In view of the homotopy equivalence (
7.6
), we obtain hyperbolic and forgetful maps on Grothendieck-Witt spaces

K(\mathscr{E},w)\simeq GW(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle H}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)\simeq K(\mathscr{E},w).

8.
Additivity Theorems and Presentation of
GW_{0}

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Recall that the poset
[n]
has a unique strict duality
i\mapsto n-i
which gives the category
S_{n}\mathcal{A}
the structure of an exact form category
(S_{n}\mathcal{A},\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
; see §
2
and
[
Sch21
, Section 6]
.
For any
i\leq j
, the map
[0]\to\operatorname{Ar}[n]:0\mapsto(i\leq j)
induces by restriction
the exact evaluation functor
\operatorname{ev}_{i,j}:(S_{n}\mathscr{E},w)\to(\mathscr{E},w):X\mapsto X_{ij}
evaluating at
i\leq j
.
The map
[0]\to\operatorname{Ar}[n]:0\mapsto(i\leq j)
preserves dualities if
j=n-i
in which case restriction along
[0]\to\operatorname{Ar}[n]
defines an non-singular exact form functor
e_{i,j}:(S_{n}\mathscr{E},w,Q)\to(\mathscr{E},w,Q):X\mapsto X_{ij}
.
Recall from Definition
7.8
the forgetful map
F:GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\to K(\mathscr{E},w)
.

Theorem 8.1

(Additivity)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Then the form functor

(\operatorname{ev}_{12},\operatorname{ev}_{01}\circ F):(S_{3}\mathscr{E},w,Q)\longrightarrow(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\times(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)

induces a weak equivalence of spaces

GW(S_{3}\mathscr{E},w,Q)\longrightarrow GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\times K(\mathscr{E},w).

Proof.

In view of Theorem
3.1
,
the proof is the same as
[
Sch10b
, Proof of Theorem 4]
replacing Lemmas 4 and 5 of

loc. cit.
by Lemmas
8.2
and
8.3
below.
∎

Denote by
\operatorname{FormCatW}
the category of exact form categories with weak equivalences where maps are the non-singular exact form functors.
Composition is composition of form functors.
Denote by
\operatorname{FormCatW}^{str}
the subcategory of exact form categories with weak equivalences that have a strict duality where maps are the non-singular exact form functors that commute with dualities and have the identity as duality compatibility maps.
Composition is composition of form functors.
The inclusion defines the functor

\operatorname{lax}:\operatorname{FormCatW}^{str}\to\operatorname{FormCatW}.

Lemma 8.2

(Strictification lemma)
.

There are a (strictification) functor

\operatorname{str}:\operatorname{FormCatW}\to\operatorname{FormCatW}^{str}:(\mathcal{A},w,*,\eta,Q)\mapsto(\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{str}}_{w},w,\sharp,id,Q^{\operatorname{str}})

and natural transformations

(\Sigma,\sigma_{q},\sigma):id\to\operatorname{lax}\circ\operatorname{str}
and
(\Lambda,\lambda_{q},\Lambda):\operatorname{lax}\circ\operatorname{str}\to id
such that the
compositions
(\Sigma,\sigma_{q},\sigma)\circ(\Lambda,\lambda_{q},\lambda)
and

(\Lambda,\lambda_{q},\lambda)\circ(\Sigma,\sigma_{q},\sigma)
are weakly equivalent to the
identity form functor.
In particular, for any exact form category with weak equivalences

(\mathscr{E},w,*,\operatorname{can},Q)
, we have a homotopy equivalence of Grothendieck-Witt spaces

GW(\Sigma,\sigma_{q},\sigma):GW(\mathscr{E},w,*,\operatorname{can},Q)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}GW(\mathscr{E}^{\operatorname{str}}_{w},w,\sharp,id,Q^{str}).

Proof.

The category with duality and weak equivalences
(\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{str}}_{w},w,\sharp,id)
was defined in
[
Sch10b
, Proof of Lemma 4]
.
We need to equip it with a quadratic functor
Q^{\operatorname{str}}:(\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{str}}_{w})^{op}\to\operatorname{Ab}
.
Recall from
loc. cit.
that the objects of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{str}}_{w}
are triples
(A,B,f)
with
A
,
B
objects of

\mathcal{A}
and
f:A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}B^{*}
a weak equivalence in
\mathcal{A}
.
A morphism from
(A_{0},B_{0},f_{0})
to
(A_{1},B_{1},f_{1})

is a pair
(a,b)
of morphisms
a:A_{0}\to A_{1}
and
b:B_{1}\to B_{0}
in
\mathcal{A}

such that
f_{1}a=b^{*}f_{0}
.
The dual of
(A,B,f)
is
(B,A,f^{*}\operatorname{can})
.
We set

Q^{\operatorname{str}}(A,B,f)=\{(\xi,a)\in Q(A)\times\mathcal{A}(A,B)|\ f^{*}\operatorname{can}\circ a=a^{*}f=\rho(\xi)\}

and define transfer and restriction as

\tau_{\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{str}}}(a,b)=(\tau_{\mathcal{A}}(b^{*}f),a+b),\hskip 12.91663pt\rho_{\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{str}}}(\xi,a)=a.

One checks that this makes
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{str}}
into an exact form category with weak equivalences.
On the level of exact categories with duality and weak equivalences, the natural transformations

(\Sigma,\sigma)
and
(\Lambda,\lambda)
where defined in
loc. cit.
.
On quadratic forms we define them as

\begin{array}[]{cccccccc}\sigma_{q}:&Q(A)&\to&Q^{\operatorname{str}}(A,A^{*},\operatorname{can})&:&\xi&\mapsto&(\xi,\rho(\xi))\\
\lambda_{q}:&Q^{\operatorname{str}}(A,B,f)&\to&Q(A)&:&(\xi,a)&\mapsto&\xi.\end{array}

The rest is as in
[
Sch10b
, Proof of Lemma 4]
.
∎

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Recall from §
2
, or
[
Sch21
, Lemma 6.2]
, that for a poset
\mathcal{P}
with strict duality, the category of functors
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{P},\mathscr{E})
is canonically an exact form category.
A sequence of functors
\mathcal{P}\to\mathscr{E}
is called exact if it is admissibly exact in
\mathscr{E}
when evaluated at each object of
\mathcal{P}
.
This makes
\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{P},\mathscr{E})
into an exact form category with weak equivalences.

As in
[
Sch10b
]
, we let
\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\mathcal{P},E)\subset\operatorname{Fun}(\mathcal{P},E)
be the full subcategory of those functors
F:\mathcal{P}\to\mathscr{E}
which send every arrow in
\mathcal{P}
to a weak equivalence in
\mathscr{E}
.
By restriction of structure,
\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\mathcal{P},\mathscr{E})
is an exact form category with weak equivalences.
In particular, for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain the exact form category with weak equivalences

\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},E)
where
\underline{n}=[n]^{op}[n]=\{n^{\prime}<\cdots 0^{\prime}<0<1<\cdots<n\}
.
For a functor

X\in\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},E)
the abelian group
Q(X)
of quadratic forms on
X
consists of those pairs
(\xi,\varphi)
where
\xi\in Q(X_{0^{\prime}})
is a quadratic form on
X_{0^{\prime}}\in\mathscr{E}
and
\varphi:X\to X^{*}
is a symmetric form such that
\rho(\xi):X_{0^{\prime}}\to(X_{0^{\prime}})^{*}
is the diagonal arrow in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{X_{0^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X(0^{\prime}\to 0)}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{X_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\textstyle{(X_{0})^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{X(0^{\prime}\to 0)^{*}}
\textstyle{(X_{0^{\prime}})^{*}.}

Varying
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we obtain a simplicial exact form category with weak equivalences
n\mapsto\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},E)
where
\underline{n}=[n]^{op}[n]
.

Lemma 8.3

(Simplicial Resolution Lemma)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,*,\eta,Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong
duality.
Then there are homotopy equivalences

\begin{array}[]{rcl}|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)|&\simeq&|n\mapsto i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},\mathscr{E}))|,\\
GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)&\simeq&|n\mapsto GW(\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},\mathscr{E}),i,Q)|\end{array}

which are functorial for exact form functors
(F,\varphi_{q},\varphi)

for which
\varphi
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

For a category
\mathcal{C}
, denote by
i\mathcal{C}
the subcategory of isomorphisms in
\mathcal{C}
.
We have an equivalence of categories

(8.1)

i\operatorname{Fun}([n]^{op},\ w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathscr{E},Q))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}i\operatorname{Quad}(\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},\mathscr{E}),Q)

which sends an object

(X_{n},\xi_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n}}}{{\to}}(X_{n-1},\xi_{n-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n-1}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}}}{{\to}}(X_{0},\xi_{0})

of the left
hand category to the object

X_{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n}}}{{\to}}X_{n-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n-1}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}}}{{\to}}X_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho(\xi_{0})}}{{\longrightarrow}}X_{0}^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{1}^{*}}}{{\to}}X_{1}^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{2}^{*}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{n}^{*}}}{{\to}}X_{n}^{*}

equipped with the form
(\xi_{0},\varphi)
where
\varphi=(\eta_{X_{n}},...,\eta_{X_{0}},1,...,1)
.
A map
(g_{n},...,g_{0})
(which is an isomorphism) is sent
to the map
(g_{n},...,g_{0},(g_{0}^{*})^{-1},...,(g_{n}^{*})^{-1}
).
Together with the weak equivalence

|\mathcal{C}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}|n\mapsto i\operatorname{Fun}([n]^{op},\mathcal{C})|

[
Sch10b
, Proof of Lemma 5, Equation (6)]
for any small category
\mathcal{C}
, this yields the horizontal homotopy equivalences in the commutative diagram

\textstyle{|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E},Q)|\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-21.0972pt\sim}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{|n\mapsto i\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},\mathscr{E}),Q)|\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F}
\textstyle{|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-24.11108pt\sim}
\textstyle{|n\mapsto i\operatorname{Quad}\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathscr{H}\operatorname{Fun}_{w}(\underline{n},\mathscr{E})|.}

The rest is as in
[
Sch10b
, Lemma 5]
.
∎

Recall that a quadratic space in an exact form category with weak equivalences
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
is a pair
(X,\xi)
of an object
X
of
\mathscr{E}
equipped with a form
\xi\in Q(X)
such that
\rho(\xi):X\to X^{\sharp}
is a weak equivalence.

Theorem 8.4

(Presentation of
GW_{0}
)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Then the abelian group

GW_{0}(\mathscr{E},w,Q)=\pi_{0}GW(\mathscr{E},w,Q)

has the following presentation.
Generators are the classes
[X,\xi]
of quadratic spaces
(X,\xi)
in
(\mathscr{E},w,Q)
.
The relations are as follows.

(1)

[X\oplus Y,\xi\perp\zeta]=[X,\xi]+[Y,\zeta]

(2)

If
g:X\to Y
is a weak equivalence, then
[Y,\xi]=[X,g^{\bullet}(\xi)]
.

(3)

If
(E_{\bullet},\xi,\varphi)
is a quadratic space in the category
of exact sequences
S_{2}\mathscr{E}
, that is,
\varphi
is a map

\textstyle{\hskip 8.61108ptE_{\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces:}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{E_{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{-1}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{E_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\hskip 8.61108ptE_{\bullet}^{\sharp}:}
\textstyle{E_{1}^{\sharp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{E_{0}^{\sharp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\sharp}}
\textstyle{E_{-1}^{\sharp}}

of admissible exact sequences in
\mathscr{E}
with

(\varphi_{-1},\varphi_{0},\varphi_{1})=(\varphi_{1}^{\sharp},\varphi_{0}^{\sharp},\varphi_{-1}^{\sharp})\circ\operatorname{can}
a symmetric
weak equivalence,
\xi\in Q(E_{0})
a quadratic form on
E_{0}
satisfying
i^{\bullet}(\xi)=0
and
\rho(\xi)=\varphi_{0}

then

[E_{0},\xi]=[H(E_{-1})].

Proof.

This is
mutatis mutandis
the same as
[
Sch10b
, Proposition 3]
reducing the proof of the theorem to the presentation of
GW_{0}
for exact form categories with strong duality (and weak equivalences the isomorphisms) of
[
Sch21
, Theorem 6.5]
by means of the Stricification and Simplicial Resolution Lemmas
8.2
and
8.3
.
∎

We finish the section with a consequence of Additivity for the homotopy type of the
n
-th iteration of the
\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}
-construction.
For an exact form category with weak equivalences
\mathscr{E}=(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
,
we write
\mathcal{R}^{(n)}_{\bullet\cdots\bullet}\mathscr{E}
for the
n
-th iterate of the
\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}
-construction.
This is the
n
-simplicial exact form category with weak equivalences

\mathcal{R}^{(n)}_{k_{1},...,k_{n}}\mathscr{E}=\mathcal{R}_{k_{1}}\mathcal{R}_{k_{2}}\dots\mathcal{R}_{k_{n}}\mathscr{E},\vskip 8.61108pt

which is the full exact form subcategory with weak equivalences of

\operatorname{Fun}(\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{1}})\times\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{2}})\times\cdots\times\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{n}}),\mathscr{E})

consisting of those functors
A:\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{1}})\times\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{2}})\times\cdots\times\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{n}})\longrightarrow\mathscr{E}

such that for all
r,s=1,...,n
and all
i_{r}\leq j_{r}\in\underline{k_{r}}
,
r\neq s
, the functor

A_{(i_{1},j_{1}),\dots(i_{s-1},j_{s-1}),\bullet,(i_{s+1},j_{s+1}),\dots(i_{n},j_{n}}):\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{s}})\to\mathscr{E}

is an object of
\mathcal{R}_{k_{s}}\mathscr{E}
.
Weak equivalences and admissible exact sequences are those maps and sequences of diagrams which are weak equivalences and admissible exact sequences when evaluated at every object of
\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{1}})\times\cdots\times\operatorname{Ar}(\underline{k_{n}})
.
We denote by
\mathcal{R}^{(n)}_{\bullet}\mathscr{E}
the diagonal of the multi-simplicial exact form category with weak equivalences
\mathcal{R}^{(n)}_{\bullet\cdots\bullet}\mathscr{E}
.

Corollary 8.5
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Then for all
n\geq 1
the form functor

(\operatorname{ev}_{0^{\prime}0},\prod_{r=0}^{n-1}\operatorname{ev}_{r,r+1}\circ F):(\mathcal{R}_{n}\mathscr{E},w,Q)\longrightarrow(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\times(\mathscr{H}\mathscr{E},w)^{n}

induces a weak equivalence of spaces after application of the functor
|w\operatorname{Quad}\mathcal{R}^{(n)}_{\bullet}|
.

Proof.

This immediately follows from Theorem
8.1
and is
mutatis mutandis
the same as
[
Sch17
, Proposition A.8]
.
∎

If we replace
\mathscr{E}
with
\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}\mathscr{E}
in the sequence (
7.4
) we obtain the sequence of pointed topological spaces

(8.2)

|w\operatorname{Quad}\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\mathscr{E},Q)|\longrightarrow|w\operatorname{Quad}\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}(\mathscr{E},Q)|\longrightarrow|wS_{\bullet}\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}\mathscr{E}|

with trivial composition.

Proposition 8.6
.

Let
\mathscr{E}=(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Then for all
n\geq 1
, the sequence
(
8.2
)
of pointed topological spaces
is a homotopy fibration.

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of
[
Sch17
, Proposition 4.1]
replacing the reference to
[
Sch10b
, §3 Remark 7]
with Corollary
8.5
.
∎

Composing the second map in (
8.2
) with the homotopy equivalence

|wS_{\bullet}\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}\mathscr{E}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}|wS_{\bullet}S_{\bullet}^{(n)}\mathscr{E}|

of
[
Sch10b
, §2.4 Lemma 1]
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 8.7
.

Let
\mathscr{E}=(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be a an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Then for every integer
n\geq 1
, the sequence

|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(n)}\mathscr{E},Q)|\longrightarrow|w\operatorname{Quad}(\mathcal{R}_{\bullet}^{(1+n)}\mathscr{E},Q)|\longrightarrow|wS_{\bullet}^{(1+n)}\mathscr{E}|

is a homotopy fibration of pointed topological spaces.

9.
The Fibration Theorem

For an exact category with weak equivalences
(\mathscr{E},w)
,
the full subcategory
\mathscr{E}^{w}
of
w
-acyclic objects is closed under extensions in
\mathscr{E}
, and thus inherits an
exact structure from
\mathscr{E}
such that the inclusion
\mathscr{E}^{w}\subset\mathscr{E}
preserves and detects admissible
exact sequences.

We recall from
[
Sch10b
, Section 4]
the definition of a symmetric cone.

Definition 9.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,*,\operatorname{can})
be an exact category with weak equivalences and duality.
A
symmetric cone
on
(\mathscr{E},w,*,\operatorname{can})
is given by the following data:

(1)

exact functors
P:\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{E}^{w}
, and
C:\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{E}^{w}
,

(2)

a natural admissible epimorphism
p_{E}:PE\twoheadrightarrow E
and a natural admissible monomorphism

i_{E}:E\rightarrowtail CE
,

(3)

a natural transformation
\gamma:P*\to*C
,

\gamma_{E}:P(E^{*})\to(CE)^{*}
, such that

i^{*}_{E}\circ\gamma_{E}=p_{E^{*}}
for all objects
E
of
\mathscr{E}
.

The symmetric cone is called
strong
if
\gamma
is a natural isomorphism.

It is convenient to define
\tilde{\gamma}_{E}:C(E^{*})\to(PE)^{*}
by

\tilde{\gamma}_{E}=P(\operatorname{can}_{E})^{*}\circ\gamma_{E^{*}}^{*}\circ\operatorname{can}_{C(E^{*})}
.
Then we have

(9.1)

\begin{array}[]{ll}i^{*}_{E}\gamma_{E}=p_{E^{*}},&\tilde{\gamma}_{E}=P(\operatorname{can}_{E})^{*}\circ\gamma_{E^{*}}^{*}\circ\operatorname{can}_{C(E^{*})},\\
p_{E}^{*}=\tilde{\gamma}_{E}\circ i_{E^{*}},&\gamma_{E}=C(\operatorname{can}_{E})^{*}\circ\tilde{\gamma}_{E^{*}}^{*}\circ\operatorname{can}_{P(E^{*})},\\
\gamma_{E^{*}}\circ P(\operatorname{can}_{E})=\tilde{\gamma}_{E}^{*}\circ\operatorname{can}_{PE},&\tilde{\gamma}_{E^{*}}\circ C(\operatorname{can}_{E})=\gamma_{E}^{*}\circ\operatorname{can}_{CE}.\end{array}

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,*,\operatorname{can})
be an exact category with weak equivalences and duality which has a symmetric cone
(C,P)
.
If in a map of admissible exact sequences (
7.1
),
two of the maps
f_{-1},f_{0},f_{1}
are weak equivalences then so is the third.
In particular, an arrow
f:X\to Y
in
\mathscr{E}
is a weak equivalence if and only if its cone

C(f)=\operatorname{colim}\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 15.35486pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-15.35486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C(X)}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 39.35486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 34.35486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 10.0pt\hbox{{}{\hbox{\kern-10.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}}}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.28532pt\raise 5.81874pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.81319pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hskip 9.04166pti_{X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.35486pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.82182pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 78.42429pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 78.42429pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)

is acyclic.
Similarily,

f:X\to Y
in
\mathscr{E}
is a weak equivalence if and only if its path object

P(f)=\lim\left(\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 7.53471pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.93225pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.53471pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 69.56248pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces P(Y)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.39279pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{p_{Y}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 45.5625pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\right)

is acyclic.

Proof.

The case where
f_{-1}
and
f_{1}
are weak equivalences is Lemma
7.1
.
So, assume
f_{-1}
and
f_{0}
are weak equivalences.
Using the same factorisation of
f_{0}
as in Lemma
7.1
we can assume that
f_{-1}
is the identity and thus,
f_{0}
is the pull-back of
f_{1}
.
Factoring
f_{1}
into a weak equivalence followed by an admissible epimorphism
X_{1}\to X_{1}\oplus P(Y_{1})\to Y_{1}
and taking the pull-back of that composition along
Y_{0}\twoheadrightarrow Y_{1}
allows us to assume that
f_{1}
is an admissible epimorphism.
Then
f_{0}
is an admissible epimorphism which is a weak equivalence.
By Lemma
7.1

\ker(f_{0})
is acyclic.
Since
\ker(f_{0})=\ker(f_{1})
, the map
f_{1}
is a weak equivalence again by Lemma
7.1
.
The case when
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
are weak equivalences is similar, and we omit the details.

The statement about the cone
C(f)
of
f
follows from the above applied to the following map of admissible exact sequences in which the middle vertical arrow is a weak equivalence

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Y\oplus C(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(1\ 0)}
\textstyle{C(f)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{1}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Similarily for the path object
P(f)
.
∎

The main result of this section is the following.

Theorem 9.3

(Fibration)
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences.
Assume that the underlying exact category with weak equivalences and duality
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can})
has a strong symmetric cone.
Let
v
be another set of weak equivalences in
\mathscr{E}
containing
w
and which
is closed under the duality.
Consider
(\mathscr{E}^{v},w)
,
(\mathscr{E}^{v},v)
,
(\mathscr{E},v)
as exact form categories with weak equivalences by restricting
(\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
.
Then the commutative square of duality preserving full inclusions of exact form categories

(9.2)

\textstyle{(\mathscr{E}^{v},w,Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{E}^{v},v,Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{E},w,Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{E},v,Q)}

induces a
homotopy cartesian square of associated
Grothendieck-Witt spaces.
Moreover, the upper right
corner has contractible Grothendieck-Witt space.

Remark 9.4
.

Theorem
9.3
was proved for symmetric forms in
[
Sch10b
, Theorem 6]
.
In
loc. cit.
we did not assume the symmetric cone to be strong but this is used in the proof as we will see below.
This does not affect any applications of
[
Sch10b
, Theorem 6]
in
[
Sch10b
]
as all exact categories with weak equivalences and duality
the theorem was applied to had a strong symmetric cone.

We will reduce the proof of the Theorem
9.3
to idempotent complete exact form categories.
Recall that
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
denotes the idempotent completion of an exact category
\mathscr{E}
; see Example
4.3
.
If
(\mathscr{E},w)
is an exact category with weak equivalences, then
\tilde{\mathscr{E}}
is equipped with the set of weak equivalences which are precisely the retracts of weak equivalences in
\mathscr{E}
.
We need the following.

Lemma 9.5
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\sharp,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong
duality which has a strong symmetric cone.
Then the
commutative diagram of exact form categories (the weak equivalences being the isomorphisms)

\textstyle{(\mathscr{E}^{w},i,Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\tilde{\mathscr{E}}^{w},i,Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{E},i,Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\tilde{\mathscr{E}},i,Q)}

induces a homotopy cartesian square of Grothendieck-Witt spaces.

Proof.

Using the Cofinality Theorem
4.2
and Lemma
4.1
, the proof is now the same as in
[
Sch10b
, Lemma 6]
.
∎

Recall that the category
\operatorname{Mor}\mathscr{E}=\operatorname{Fun}([1],\mathscr{E})
of morphisms in an exact form category
(\mathscr{E},*,\operatorname{can},Q)
,
is equipped with the quadratic functor
Q
which at the object
g:X\to Y
of
\operatorname{Mor}\mathscr{E}
consists of all pairs
(\xi,a)
where
\xi\in Q(X)
and
a:X\to Y^{*}
such that
ag^{*}=a^{*}\operatorname{can}_{Y}g=\rho(\xi)
.
By restriction, this makes
\operatorname{Mor}_{w}\mathscr{E}=\operatorname{Fun}_{w}([1],\mathscr{E})
into an exact form category.
As in
[
Sch10b
, Section 4]
the following proposition is the key to proving
Theorem
9.3
.

Proposition 9.6
.

Let
(\mathscr{E},w,\ast,\operatorname{can},Q)
be an exact form category with weak equivalences and strong
duality.
Assume that
(\mathscr{E},w,\ast,\operatorname{can})
has a strong symmetric cone.
Then the commutative diagram

(9.3)

\textstyle{({\mathscr{E}}^{w},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\operatorname{Mor}_{w}}{\mathscr{E}}^{w},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{({\mathscr{E}},Q)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-12.05554ptI}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-6.02777ptE\mapsto 1_{E}}
\textstyle{({\operatorname{Mor}}_{w}{\mathscr{E}},Q)}

of full inclusions of exact form categories with strong duality (all weak
equivalences being isomorphisms)
induces a homotopy cartesian square of associated Grothendieck-Witt spaces.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.5
, we can assume that all categories in diagram (
9.3
) are idempotent complete.
We will write
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathscr{E},w)
and
\mathcal{C}(\mathscr{E},w)
for
\mathcal{C}_{0}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E}^{w})
and
\mathcal{C}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E}^{w})
.
As in
[
Sch10b
, Proof of Proposition 4]
,
we will construct a commutative diagram of
exact form categories with strong duality and non-singular exact form functors

(9.4)

\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}^{w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Mor}_{w}}{\mathscr{E}}^{w}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}({\mathscr{E}}^{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{C}}({\operatorname{Mor}_{w}}{\mathscr{E}}^{w})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathscr{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip-4.52083ptI}
